    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 215}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. STRACATHRO}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item ALAS, and did my Saviour bleed, \\
            And did my Sovereign die? \\
        Did He devote that sacred head \\
            For such a worm as I?

    \item Was it for sins that I had done \\
            He groaned upon the tree? \\
        Amazing pity, grace unknown, \\
            And love beyond degree!

    \item Well might the sun in darkness hide, \\
            And shut its glories in, \\
        When the incarnate Maker died \\
            For man, His creature's sin.

    \item Thus might I hide my blushing face \\
            While Calvary's cross appears; \\
        Dissolve my heart in thankfulness, \\
            And melt mine eyes to tears.

    \item But drops of grief can ne'er repay \\
            The debt of love I owe; \\
        Fain would I give myself away; \\
            'Tis all that I can do.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 216}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D. PASSION CHORALE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O SACRED Head once wounded, \\
            With grief and pain weighed down, \\
        How scornfully surrounded \\
            With thorns, Thine only crown! \\
        How art Thou pale with anguish, \\
            With sore abuse and scorn! \\
        How does that visage languish, \\
            Which once was bright as morn!

    \item O sacred Head, what glory, \\
            What bliss till now was Thine! \\
        Yet, though despised and gory, \\
            I joy to call Thee mine. \\
        Thy grief and Thy compassion \\
            Were all for sinners' gain; \\
        Mine, mine was the transgression, \\
            But Thine the deadly pain.

    \item What language shall I borrow, \\
            To praise Thee, heavenly Friend, \\
        For this Thy dying sorrow, \\
            Thy pity without end? \\
        Lord, I am Thine for ever, \\
            Ne'er let me faithless prove; \\
        Oh, let me never, never, \\
            Forget such dying love! Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 217}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. ST. VALENTINE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item TO Calvary, Lord, in spirit oft \\
            Our weary souls repair, \\
        To dwell upon Christ's dying love, \\
            And taste its sweetness there.

    \item Sweet resting place of every heart \\
            That feels the plague of sin, \\
        Yet knows that deep mysterious joy, \\
            The peace of God within.

    \item There, through that hour of deepest woe, \\
            His suffering spirit passed; \\
        Grace there its wondrous victory gained, \\
            And love endured its last.

    \item Dear suffering Lamb! His grievous wounds \\
            Have drawn, with cords of love, \\
        Our willing hearts to Him, and linked \\
            Our life with His above.

    \item His sympathies and hopes are ours; \\
            O God, we wait to see \\
        Creation all, below, above, \\
            By Him redeemed and free.

    \item Our longing eyes would fain behold \\
            That bright and bless\`ed brow, \\
        Once wrung with bitterest anguish, wear \\
            Its crown of glory now.

    \item Why linger then? Come, Saviour come, \\
            Responsive to our call; \\
        Come, claim Thy rightful power and reign, \\
            The Heir and Lord of all.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 218}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular HE DIES!}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HE dies! He dies! the lowly Man of Sorrows, \\
        On whom were laid our many griefs and woes; \\
        Our sins He bore beneath God's awful billows, \\
        And He hath triumphed over all our foes.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{I am He that liveth, that liveth and was dead;} \\
    \textit{I am He that liveth, that liveth and was dead;} \\
    \textit{    And behold! I am alive for evermore,} \\
    \textit{    Behold! I am alive for evermore.} \\
    \textit{I am He that liveth, that liveth and was dead;} \\
    \textit{    And behold! I am alive for evermore.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item He lives! He lives! what glorious consolation! \\
        Exalted at His Father's own right hand \\
        He pleads for us, and by His intercession \\
        Enables all His saints by grace to stand.

    \item He comes! He comes! Oh, blest anticipation! \\
        In keeping with His true and faithful word, \\
        To call us to our heavenly consummation--- \\
        Caught up, to be for ever with the Lord.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 219}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. ALLHALLOWS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WHAT sacred fountain yonder springs \\
            Out from God's throne above, \\
        And all our covenant blessing brings? \\
            'Tis God's unbounded love.

    \item What mighty sum paid all my debt \\
            When charged with guilt I stood, \\
        And hath my soul at freedom set? \\
            'Tis Jesus' precious blood.

    \item What stream of light doth now dispel \\
            My terror of the rod? \\
        Forth from the cross my fears to quell \\
            Shines bright the love of God.

    \item What voice is that which speaks for me \\
            In heaven's high court for good, \\
        And from the curse hath set me free? \\
            'Tis Jesus' precious blood.

    \item What themes will fill our hearts above \\
            When praise we sing to God? \\
        His own surpassing boundless love \\
            And Jesus' precious blood.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 220}}
    \\
    \textit{S. M. SWABIA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item CHRIST'S grave is vacant now, \\
            Left for the throne above; \\
        His cross asserts God's right to bless \\
            In His own boundless love.

    \item 'Twas there the blood was shed, \\
            'Twas there the life was poured; \\
        There Mercy gained her diadem, \\
            While Justice sheathed her sword.

    \item And thence the child of faith \\
            Sees judgment all gone by, \\
        Perceives the sentence fully met, \\
            "The soul that sins shall die;"

    \item Learns how that God in love \\
            Gave Christ, the sins to bear \\
        Of all who own His lordship now, \\
            That they His place may share;

    \item And cries with wondering joy, \\
            "As He is, so am I--- \\
        Pure, holy, loved as Christ Himself; \\
            Who shall my peace destroy?

    \item "Reach my blest Saviour first, \\
            Take Him from God's esteem; \\
        Prove Jesus bears one spot of sin, \\
            Then tell me I'm unclean.

    \item "Nay, for He purged my guilt \\
            With His own precious blood; \\
        And such its virtue, not a stain \\
            E'er meets the eye of God."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 221}}
    \\
    \textit{L. M. MARYTON}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HE lives, the great Redeemer lives \\
            (What joy the blest assurance gives!) \\
        And now before His Father, God, \\
            Pleads the full merit of His blood.

    \item Great Advocate, almighty Friend, \\
            On Him do all our hopes depend; \\
        Our cause can never, never fail, \\
            For He doth plead, and must prevail.

    \item In every dark, distressing hour, \\
            When sin and Satan join their power, \\
        Let this blest truth repel each dart--- \\
            That Christ doth bear us on His heart.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 222}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. ARNOLD.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item IN heaven, before God's holy face, \\
            Behold an Advocate \\
        Who intercedes for us in grace, \\
            With power divinely great.

    \item He loves us deeply, this we know \\
            By tokens that He wears; \\
        Pierced hands and feet and side, all show \\
            The matchless love He bears.

    \item He died to ransom us from sin, \\
            He lives to save us still \\
        From every power without, within, \\
            That seeks to work us ill.

    \item He, touched with trials that attend \\
            The weakness of our frame, \\
        Can sympathetic succour lend, \\
            For He has known the same.

    \item He overcame His mightiest foe, \\
            When He earth's pathway trod; \\
        And victory we may also know, \\
            Who come through Him to God.

    \item Fear not to come, if sin o'ertake, \\
            Confessing all to God, \\
        Who will forgive us for His sake, \\
            And cleanse us by His blood.

    \item Christ will fresh strength for conflict give, \\
            The needed grace impart, \\
        That each below for Him may live, \\
            And He live in each heart.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 223}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. ST. AGNES, DURHAM.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WITH joy we meditate the grace \\
            Of our High Priest above; \\
        His heart is filled with tenderness, \\
            His very name is Love.

    \item Touched with a sympathy within, \\
            He knows our feeble frame; \\
        He knows what sore temptations are, \\
            For He has felt the same.

    \item But spotless, undefiled and pure, \\
            Our great Redeemer stood; \\
        While Satan's fiery darts He bore, \\
            And did resist to blood.

    \item He in the days of lowly flesh \\
            Poured out His cries and tears; \\
        And, though exalted, feels afresh \\
            What every member bears.

    \item Then boldly let our faith address \\
            His mercy and His power; \\
        We shall obtain delivering grace \\
            In each distressing hour.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 224}}
    \\
    \textit{L. M. TRURO.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THE Saviour lives, no more to die; \\
        He lives, our Head, enthroned on high; \\
        He lives triumphant o'er the grave; \\
        He lives eternally to save.

    \item He lives to still His people's fears; \\
        He lives to wipe away their tears; \\
        He lives their mansions to prepare; \\
        He lives to bring them safely there.

    \item Then let our souls in Him rejoice, \\
        And sing His praise with cheerful voice; \\
        Our doubts and fears for ever gone, \\
        For Christ is on the Father's throne.

    \item The chief of sinners He receives, \\
        His saints He loves and never leaves; \\
        He'll guard us safe from every ill, \\
        And all His promises fulfil.

    \item Abundant grace He will afford \\
        Till we are present with the Lord, \\
        And prove what we have sung before, \\
        Christ Jesus lives for evermore.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 225}}
    \\
    \textit{L. M. DUKE STREET.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WHERE high the heavenly temple stands, \\
        The house of God not made with hands, \\
        A great High Priest true manhood wears, \\
        And there before our God appears.

    \item He, who for us as surety stood, \\
        Yielding on earth His precious blood, \\
        Pursues in heaven His gracious plan, \\
        The Saviour and the Friend of man.

    \item Partaker of the human name, \\
        He knows the frailty of our frame, \\
        And still remembers in the skies \\
        His tears and griefs and agonies.

    \item In every pang that rends the heart \\
        The Man of Sorrows bears a part; \\
        He knows and feels our every grief, \\
        And gives the suffering saint relief.

    \item With boldness therefore at the throne \\
        Let us make all our sorrows known, \\
        And seek the aid of heavenly power \\
        To help us in each trying hour.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 226}}
    \\
    \textit{L. M. SILVER HILL.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item BEFORE the throne of God above \\
        I have a strong, a perfect plea--- \\
        A great High Priest, whose name is Love, \\
        Who ever lives and pleads for me.

    \item My name is graven on His hands; \\
        My name is written on His heart; \\
        I know that while in heaven He stands, \\
        No tongue can bid me hence depart.

    \item When Satan tempts me to despair \\
        And tells me of the guilt within, \\
        Upward I look and see Him there \\
        Who made an end of all my sin.

    \item Because the sinless Saviour died \\
        My sinful soul is counted free, \\
        For God, the Just, is satisfied \\
        To look on Him and pardon me.

    \item Behold Him there, the risen Lamb, \\
        My perfect, spotless righteousness; \\
        The great unchangeable I AM, \\
        The King of glory and of grace.

    \item One with Himself, I cannot die; \\
        My soul is purchased with His blood; \\
        My life is hid with Christ on high, \\
        With Christ my Saviour, and my God.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 227}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D. ST. WINIFRED}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LAMB of God, Thou now art seated \\
            High upon Thy Father's throne, \\
        All Thy gracious work completed, \\
            All Thy mighty victory won. \\
        Every knee in heaven is bending \\
            To the Lamb for sinners slain; \\
        Every voice and harp is swelling, \\
            "Worthy is the Lamb to reign."

    \item Lord, in all Thy power and glory, \\
            Still Thy thoughts and eyes are here, \\
        Watching o'er Thy ransomed people, \\
            To Thy gracious heart so dear; \\
        Thou for us art interceding \\
            (Everlasting is Thy love), \\
        And a bless\`ed rest preparing \\
            In our Father's house above.

    \item Lamb of God, Thou soon in glory \\
            Wilt to this sad earth return; \\
        All Thy foes shall quake before Thee, \\
            All that now despise Thee, mourn. \\
        Then Thy saints appearing with Thee, \\
            With Thee in Thy kingdom reign; \\
        Thine the praise and Thine the glory, \\
            Lamb of God for sinners slain! Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 228}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. WARWICK.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O GOD, 'tis joy to look above \\
            And see Christ on Thy throne; \\
        To search the heights and depths of love \\
            Which He to us has shown;

    \item To look beyond the long dark night \\
            And hail the coming day, \\
        When He to all the saints in light \\
            His glories will display.

    \item And oh, 'tis joy the path to trace \\
            By Him so meekly trod; \\
        Learning of Him to walk in grace, \\
            In fellowship with God.

    \item Joy to confess His bless\`ed name, \\
            The virtues of His blood; \\
        And to the weary heart proclaim, \\
            "Behold the Lamb of God."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 229}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. JAZER.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O BLEST Lord Jesus, who but Thou \\
            On earth, in heaven above, \\
        May claim from all our willing hearts \\
            The full response of love?

    \item We love Thy brethren, Lord, 'tis true, \\
            Because in them we see \\
        Sweet traces of Thy bless\`ed self, \\
            As here they walk with Thee.

    \item But oh! Thou loving One, 'twas Thine, \\
            Thine only, Lord, to part \\
        With life, and all that love could give, \\
            To win the wandering heart.

    \item Thus, heirs of endless bliss with Thee, \\
            We love Thee and adore, \\
        And ask Thee, Lord, that we may learn \\
            To love Thee more and more.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 230}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. LYNTON.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O BLESS\`ED Saviour, is Thy love \\
            So vast, so full, so free? \\
        Fain would we give our hearts, our minds, \\
            Our lives, our all, to Thee.

    \item We love Thee for the glorious worth \\
            Which in Thyself we see; \\
        We love Thee for the shameful cross, \\
            Endured so patiently.

    \item No man of greater love can boast \\
            Than for his friend to die; \\
        Thou for Thine enemies wast slain; \\
            What love with Thine can vie?

    \item Though in the very form of God, \\
            With heavenly glory crowned, \\
        Thou didst partake of blood and flesh, \\
            Beset with sorrows round.

    \item Thou wouldst like sinful man be made, \\
            In everything but sin, \\
        That we as like Thee might become, \\
            As we unlike have been;

    \item Like Thee in faith, in meekness, love, \\
            In every heavenly grace; \\
        From glory unto glory changed \\
            Till we behold Thy face.

    \item O Lord, we treasure in our hearts \\
            The memory of Thy love; \\
        And ever may Thy name to us \\
            A grateful odour prove. Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 231}}
    \\
    \textit{L. M. OTTERBOURNE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THY dying love, O Lord, I own, \\
        A love unfathomed and unknown; \\
        All other love can measured be, \\
        But not Thy boundless love to me.

    \item Oh, wonder to myself I am, \\
        That I can view the dying Lamb, \\
        Can scan the wondrous mystery o'er, \\
        And not be moved to love Him more!

    \item 'Tis well indeed that 'twas His love, \\
        Not mine, that brought Him from above; \\
        And well that 'twas His bitter grief, \\
        Not mine, that gave my soul relief.

    \item Oh, I am weary of my love, \\
        That doth so little towards Him move! \\
        Yet do I constant, inly groan \\
        To know the depths of all His own.

    \item Loved and for ever on the throne, \\
        Adored and loved, the timeless One \\
        Will through one long eternal day \\
        The height and depth of all display.

    \item Whilst here below meanwhile I'd be \\
        Content to have Him near to me; \\
        To know but this, His love alone, \\
        And count my love too mean to own.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 232}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. SWEET IS THE PROMISE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SWEET is the promise, "I will not forget thee;" \\
        Nothing can molest or turn my soul away. \\
        E'en though the night be dark within the valley, \\
        Just beyond is shining an eternal day.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{"I will not forget thee or leave thee;} \\
    \textit{In My hands I'll hold thee, in My arms I'll fold thee;} \\
    \textit{I will not forget thee or leave thee---} \\
    \textit{I am thy Redeemer, I will care for thee."}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item How can I show my gratitude to Jesus \\
        For His love unfailing and His tender care? \\
        I will proclaim to others His salvation, \\
        That they may accept Him and His promise share.

    \item Trusting the promise, "I will not forget thee," \\
        Onward will I go with songs of joy and praise. \\
        Though earth despise me, though my friends forsake me, \\
        Jesus will be near me, gladd\`ening my days.

    \item When at the golden portals I am standing, \\
        All my tribulations, all my sorrows past, \\
        How sweet to hear the bless\`ed proclamation--- \\
        "Enter faithful servant, welcome home at last!"

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 233}}
    \\
    \textit{6.4.6.4.6.6.6.4. WORSHIP}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item "JESUS," that name we love, \\
            Jesus our Lord; \\
        "Jesus," all names above, \\
            Jesus the Lord. \\
        Thou, Lord, our all must be, \\
        Nothing that's good have we, \\
        Nothing apart from Thee, \\
            Jesus our Lord.

    \item As Son of Man it was, \\
            Jesus the Lord, \\
        Thou gav'st Thy life for us, \\
            Jesus our Lord. \\
        Great was indeed Thy love, \\
        All other love above, \\
        Love Thou didst dearly prove, \\
            Jesus our Lord.

    \item Righteous alone in Thee, \\
            Jesus our Lord; \\
        Thou wilt a refuge be, \\
            Jesus the Lord. \\
        Whom then have we to fear, \\
        What trouble, grief or care, \\
        Since Thou art ever near, \\
            Jesus our Lord?

    \item Soon Thou wilt come again, \\
            Jesus the Lord; \\
        We shall be happy then, \\
            Jesus our Lord; \\
        When Thine own face we see, \\
        Then we shall like Thee be, \\
        Then evermore with Thee, \\
            Jesus our Lord.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 234}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D PRYSGOL}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus Christ, our Saviour, \\
            We only look to Thee; \\
        'Tis in Thy love and favour \\
            Our souls find liberty. \\
        Whilst Satan fiercely rages \\
            And shipwreck oft we fear, \\
        'Tis this our grief assuages, \\
            That Thou art always near.

    \item Yea, though the tempest round us \\
            Seems safety to defy, \\
        Though rocks and shoals surround us, \\
            And swell the billows high, \\
        Thou dost through all protect us \\
            And cheer us by Thy love; \\
        Thy counsels too direct us \\
            Safe to the rest above.

    \item There with what joy reviewing \\
            Past conflicts, dangers, fears, \\
        Thy hand our foes subduing, \\
            And drying all our tears; \\
        Our hearts with rapture burning, \\
            The path we shall retrace, \\
        Where now our souls are learning \\
            The riches of Thy grace.

    \item Oh, then how loud the chorus \\
            Shall to Thy name resound, \\
        From all at rest before us, \\
            From all Thy grace hath found! \\
        One joyful song for ever \\
            Each heart, each lip, shall raise--- \\
        The praise of our Redeemer, \\
            Our God and Saviour's praise!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 235}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D LYMINGTON}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LORD, who now art seated \\
            Above the heavens on high, \\
        The gracious work completed, \\
            For which Thou cam'st to die, \\
        To Thee our hearts are lifted, \\
            While pilgrims wandering here, \\
        For Thou alone art gifted \\
            Our every weight to bear.

    \item We know that Thou hast bought us \\
            And cleansed us by Thy blood; \\
        We know Thy grace has brought us \\
            As kings and priests to God: \\
        We know that soon the morning \\
            Long looked for hasteth near, \\
        When we, at Thy returning, \\
            In glory shall appear.

    \item O Lord, Thy love's unbounded, \\
            So full, so vast, so free! \\
        Our thoughts are all confounded \\
            Whene'er we think of Thee. \\
        For us Thou cam'st from heav\`en, \\
            For us to bleed and die; \\
        That, purchased and forgiv\`en, \\
            We might ascend on high.

    \item Oh, let this love constrain us \\
            To give our hearts to Thee; \\
        Let nothing henceforth pain us \\
            But that which paineth Thee; \\
        Our joy, our one endeavour, \\
            Through suffering, conflict, shame, \\
        To serve Thee, gracious Saviour, \\
            And magnify Thy name.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 236}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular YERBURY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THY love we own, Lord Jesus, \\
        In service unremitting; \\
            Within the veil \\
            Thou dost prevail, \\
        Each soul for service fitting. \\
        Encompassed here with failure, \\
        Each earthly refuge fails us; \\
            Without, within, \\
            Beset with sin, \\
        Thy name alone avails us.

    \item Thy love we own, Lord Jesus, \\
        For, though Thy toils are ended, \\
            Thy tender heart \\
            Doth take its part \\
        With those Thy grace befriended. \\
        Thy sympathy how precious! \\
        Thou succourest in sorrow, \\
            And bid'st us cheer \\
            While pilgrims here, \\
        And haste the hopeful morrow.

    \item Thy love we own, Lord Jesus; \\
        Thy way is traced before Thee--- \\
            Thou wilt descend \\
            And we ascend \\
        To meet in heavenly glory. \\
        Soon shall the blissful morning \\
        Call forth Thy saints to meet Thee, \\
            Our only Lord, \\
            Alone adored; \\
        With gladness then we'll greet Thee.

    \item Thy love we own, Lord Jesus, \\
        And wait to see Thy glory; \\
            To know as known \\
            And fully own \\
        Thy perfect grace before Thee. \\
        We plead Thy parting promise, \\
        Come, Saviour, to release us; \\
            Then endless praise \\
            Our lips shall raise \\
        For love like Thine, Lord Jesus.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 237}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular BISHOPGARTH}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THY name we bless, Lord Jesus--- \\
        That name all names excelling. \\
            How great Thy love \\
            (All praise above) \\
        Should every tongue be telling. \\
        The Father's loving kindness \\
        In giving Thee was shown us, \\
            Now by Thy blood \\
            Redeemed to God, \\
        As children He doth own us.

    \item From that eternal glory \\
        Thou hadst with God the Father, \\
            He sent His Son \\
            That He in one \\
        His people all might gather. \\
        Our sins were all laid on Thee, \\
        God's wrath Thou hast endur\`ed; \\
            It was for us \\
            Thou suffer'dst thus, \\
        And hast our peace secur\`ed.

    \item Thou from the dead wast rais\`ed, \\
        And from all condemnation \\
            The Church is free, \\
            As raised in Thee, \\
        Head of the new creation. \\
        On high Thou hast ascended \\
        To God's right hand in heav\`en; \\
            The Lamb once slain \\
            Alive again, \\
        To Thee all power is given.

    \item Thou hast bestowed the earnest \\
        Of that we shall inherit; \\
            Till Thou shalt come \\
            To take us home \\
        We're sealed by God the Spirit. \\
        We wait for Thine appearing, \\
        When we shall know more fully \\
            The grace divine \\
            That made us Thine, \\
        Thou Lamb of God most holy.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 238}}
    \\
    \textit{8.4.8.4.8.8.8.4. CARITAS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item ONE there is above all others, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        His is love beyond a brother's, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        Earthly friends may fail or leave us, \\
        One day soothe, the next day grieve us, \\
        But this Friend will ne'er deceive us; \\
            Oh how He loves!

    \item 'Tis eternal life to know Him, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        Think, oh think how much we owe Him, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        With His precious blood He bought us, \\
        In the wilderness He sought us, \\
        To His fold He safely brought us; \\
            Oh how He loves!

    \item What a Friend is our Lord Jesus, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        'Tis His great delight to bless us, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        How our hearts delight to hear Him \\
        Bid us dwell in safety near Him! \\
        Why should we distrust or fear Him? \\
            Oh how He loves!

    \item Through His name we are forgiv\`en, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        Backward shall our foes be driv\`en, \\
            Oh how He loves! \\
        Best of blessings He'll provide us, \\
        Nought but good shall e'er betide us, \\
        Safe to glory He will guide us, \\
            Oh how He loves!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 239}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D ROOM FOR JESUS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SON of God, 'twas love that made Thee \\
            Die, our ruined souls to save; \\
        'Twas our sins' vast load that laid Thee, \\
            Lord of life, within the grave. \\
        But Thy glorious resurrection \\
            Showed Thee conqueror o'er the tomb; \\
        So the saints, by Thy protection, \\
            Through Thy work shall overcome.

    \item Thou to heaven hast now ascended, \\
            Entering there by Thine own blood, \\
        All Thy work of suffering ended, \\
            Fully wrought the will of God. \\
        For Thy saints Thou still art caring, \\
            For us pleading in Thy love, \\
        And our place of rest preparing \\
            In the Father's house above.

    \item Now the Paraclete doth gather \\
            To Thy name Thy people here; \\
        So we worship God the Father, \\
            Serve in love and holy fear. \\
        What a debt of love we owe Thee, \\
            Love that we can ne'er express, \\
        Since we through the Spirit know Thee, \\
            Christ the Lord, our righteousness!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 240}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. ARTAXERXES}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus Christ, the thought of Thee \\
        With sweetness fills my breast; \\
        But better far Thy face to see, \\
        And in Thy presence rest.

    \item Nor voice can sing, nor heart can frame, \\
        Nor can the memory find \\
        A sweeter sound than Thy blest name, \\
        O Saviour of mankind.

    \item O Hope of every contrite heart, \\
        Thou joy of all the meek, \\
        To those who fall how kind Thou art, \\
        How good to those who seek!

    \item But what to those who find! Ah, this \\
        Nor tongue, nor pen can show; \\
        Thy love, Lord Jesus, what it is \\
        None but Thy loved ones know.

    \item Our only joy and strength be Thou, \\
        As Thou our crown wilt be; \\
        Be Thou, O Lord, our glory now \\
        And through eternity. Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 241}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7. ST. BEES}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HARK, my soul, it is the Lord, \\
        'Tis thy Saviour, hear His word; \\
        Now He speaks, and speaks to thee, \\
        "Ransomed sinner, lovest thou Me?"

    \item "I delivered thee when bound, \\
        And when wounded healed thy wound, \\
        Sought thee wandering, set thee right, \\
        Turned thy darkness into light.

    \item "Can a mother's tender care \\
        Cease toward the child she bare? \\
        Yes, she may forgetful be, \\
        Yet will I remember thee.

    \item "Mine is an unchanging love, \\
        Higher than the heights above, \\
        Deeper than the depths beneath, \\
        Free and faithful, strong as death.

    \item "Thou shalt see My glory soon, \\
        When the work of grace is done; \\
        Partner of My throne shalt be--- \\
        Ransomed sinner, lovest thou Me?"

    \item Lord, it is my chief complaint \\
        That my love is weak and faint, \\
        Yet I love Thee and adore; \\
        Oh for grace to love Thee more!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 242}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular AGAPE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus, Lord Jesus, \\
            How vast Thy love to me! \\
        I'll bathe in its full ocean \\
            To all eternity, \\
        And wending on to glory \\
            This all my song shall be, \\
        "I was a guilty sinner, \\
            But Thou hast died for me."

    \item Oh Calv'ry, oh Calv'ry, \\
            The thorn-crown and the spear--- \\
        'Tis here Thy love, Lord Jesus, \\
            Thy grievous wounds appear. \\
        What depths of grace and mercy \\
            So richly flow from Thee! \\
        I was a guilty sinner, \\
            But Thou hast died for me.

    \item Adore Him, adore Him; \\
            The glorious work is done. \\
        Sin's judgment ne'er will harm me; \\
            'Twas laid upon God's Son. \\
        He suffering cried, "'Tis finished," \\
            And I my pardon see; \\
        I was a guilty sinner, \\
            But Thou hast died for me.

    \item In glory, in glory, \\
            For ever with the Lord, \\
        I with the heavenly chorus \\
            Shall sing with sweet accord; \\
        And as I gaze upon Him, \\
            My gladsome song shall be \\
        Melodious with the praises \\
            Of Christ who died for me.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 243}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D SALVATORI}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LORD, Thy love's unbounded, \\
            So sweet, so full, so free; \\
        My soul is all transported \\
            Whene'er I think of Thee. \\
        Yet, Lord, alas! what weakness \\
            Within myself I find; \\
        No infant's changing pleasure \\
            Is like my wandering mind.

    \item And yet Thy love's unchanging, \\
            And doth recall my heart \\
        To joy in all its brightness, \\
            The peace its beams impart. \\
        Yet, sure, if in Thy presence \\
            My soul still constant were, \\
        Mine eye would, more familiar, \\
            Its brighter glories bear.

    \item And thus Thy deep perfections \\
            Much better should I know, \\
        And with adoring fervour \\
            In this Thy nature grow. \\
        Still sweet 'tis to discover, \\
            If clouds have dimmed my sight, \\
        When passed, eternal Lover, \\
            As ever, Thou art bright.

    \item Oh, keep my soul, Lord Jesus, \\
            Abiding still with Thee; \\
        And if I wander, teach me \\
            Soon back to Thee to flee. \\
        That all Thy gracious favour \\
            May to my soul be known; \\
        And versed in this Thy goodness, \\
            My hopes Thyself shalt crown.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 244}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D JOY IN SORROW}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate. The last two lines of each verse to be repeated.]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I'VE found a joy in sorrow, \\
            A secret balm for pain, \\
        A beautiful to-morrow \\
            Of sunshine after rain; \\
        I've found a branch of healing \\
            Near every bitter spring, \\
        A whispered promise stealing \\
            O'er every broken string.

    \item I've found a glad hosanna \\
            For every woe and wail, \\
        A handful of sweet manna \\
            When grapes of Eshcol fail; \\
        I've found a Rock of ages \\
            When desert wells are dry, \\
        And after weary stages \\
            I've found an Elim nigh;

    \item An Elim with its coolness, \\
            Its fountains and its shade; \\
        A blessing in its fulness, \\
            When buds of promise fade. \\
        O'er tears of sweet contrition \\
            I've seen a rainbow light, \\
        A glory and fruition, \\
            So near though out of sight.

    \item Lord Jesus, Thee possessing, \\
            I have the joy, the balm, \\
        The healing and the blessing, \\
            The sunshine and the psalm, \\
        The promise for the fearful, \\
            The Elim for the faint, \\
        The rainbow for the tearful, \\
            The glory for the saint.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 245}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7. D. HOLLINGSIDE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JESUS, Lover of my soul, \\
            Let me to Thy bosom fly, \\
        While the raging billows roll, \\
            While the tempest still is high. \\
        Hide me, O my Saviour, hide, \\
            Till the storm of life is o'er; \\
        Safe into the haven guide, \\
            Where the tempest's heard no more.

    \item Other refuge have I none, \\
            Hangs my helpless soul on Thee; \\
        Leave, ah, leave me not alone, \\
            Still support and comfort me. \\
        All my trust on Thee is stayed, \\
            All my help from Thee I bring; \\
        Cover my defenceless head \\
            With the shadow of Thy wing.

    \item Thou, O Christ, art all I want, \\
            More than all in Thee I find; \\
        Raise the fallen, cheer the faint, \\
            Heal the sick and lead the blind. \\
        Just and holy is Thy name, \\
            I am all unrighteousness, \\
        I am full of sin and shame, \\
            Thou art full of truth and grace.

    \item Plenteous grace with Thee is found, \\
            Grace to pardon all my sin; \\
        Let the healing streams abound, \\
            Make and keep me pure within. \\
        Thou of life the fountain art, \\
            Freely let me take of Thee; \\
        Spring Thou up within my heart, \\
            Rise to all eternity.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 246}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.6.8.8.6. PURLEIGH}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LOVE divine, how sweet thou art! \\
        When shall I find my willing heart \\
            All taken up by thee? \\
        Oh, may I pant and thirst to prove \\
        The greatness of redeeming love, \\
            The love of God to me!

    \item God only knows the love of God; \\
        Oh, that it more were shed abroad \\
            In this poor heart of mine! \\
        For love I sigh, for love I pine; \\
        This only portion, Lord, be mine, \\
            Be mine the better part.

    \item Oh, that I may for ever sit, \\
        Like Mary, at the Master's feet--- \\
            Be this my happy choice; \\
        My only care, my only bliss, \\
        My joy, my heaven on earth be this, \\
            To hear the Bridegroom's voice.

    \item Oh, that I may, like favoured John, \\
        Recline my wearied head upon \\
            The great Redeemer's breast! \\
        From care and sin and sorrow free, \\
        Give me, O Lord, to find in Thee \\
            My everlasting rest.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 247}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.8.8. TRANQUILLITY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus, Thou who only art \\
        The endless source of purest joy, \\
        Oh come and fill this longing heart, \\
        May nought but Thou my thoughts employ. \\
        Teach me on Thee to fix mine eye, \\
        For none but Thou can satisfy.

    \item The joys of earth can never fill \\
        The heart that's tasted of Thy love; \\
        No portion would I seek until \\
        I reign with Thee, my Lord, above; \\
        When I shall gaze upon Thy face, \\
        And know more fully all Thy grace.

    \item When from the radiant throne on high \\
        Thou didst my fall and ruin see, \\
        Thou cam'st to earth for me to die \\
        That I might share Thy throne with Thee; \\
        Loved with an everlasting love, \\
        My hopes, my joys are all above.

    \item Oh what is all that earth can give? \\
        I'm called to share in God's own joy! \\
        Dead to the world, in Thee I live, \\
        In Thee I've bliss without alloy; \\
        Well may I earthly joys resign, \\
        All things are mine and I am Thine.

    \item Till Thou shalt come to take me home, \\
        Be this my one ambition, Lord, \\
        Self, sin, the world to overcome, \\
        Fast clinging to Thy faithful word; \\
        More of Thyself each day to know, \\
        And more into Thine image grow.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 248}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.8.8. ST. CATHERINE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item OH, draw me, Saviour, after Thee! \\
        So shall I run and never tire; \\
        With gracious words still comfort me, \\
        Be Thou my hope, my sole desire. \\
        Free me from every weight. Nor fear \\
        Nor sin can come if Thou art near.

    \item What in Thy love possess I not? \\
        My star by night, my sun by day, \\
        My spring of life when parched with drought, \\
        My wine to cheer, my bread to stay, \\
        My strength, my shield, my safe abode, \\
        My robe before the throne of God.

    \item From all eternity with love \\
        Unchangeable Thou hast me viewed; \\
        Ere knew this beating heart to move, \\
        Thy tender mercies me pursued; \\
        Ever with me may they abide, \\
        And close me in on every side. Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 249}}
    \\
    \textit{10.10.10.10.4. IT PASSETH KNOWLEDGE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item IT passeth knowledge, that dear love of Thine, \\
        Lord Jesus, Saviour; yet this soul of mine \\
        Would of Thy love, in all its breadth and length, \\
        Its height and depth, its everlasting strength, \\
            Know more and more.

    \item It passeth telling, that dear love of Thine, \\
        Lord Jesus, Saviour; yet these lips of mine \\
        Would fain proclaim to sinners, far and near, \\
        A love which can remove all guilty fear, \\
            And love beget.

    \item It passeth praises, that dear love of Thine, \\
        Lord Jesus, Saviour; yet this heart of mine \\
        Would sing that love, so full, so rich, so free, \\
        Which brings a rebel sinner, even me, \\
            Nigh unto God.

    \item But though I cannot sing or tell or know \\
        The fulness of Thy love while here below, \\
        My empty vessel I may freely bring; \\
        O Thou, who art of love the living spring, \\
            My vessel fill!

    \item Oh, fill me, holy Saviour, with Thy love; \\
        Lead, lead me to the living fount above. \\
        Thither may I in simple faith draw nigh, \\
        And never to another fountain fly, \\
            But unto Thee.

    \item And when, Lord Jesus, Thine own face I see, \\
        When at Thy lofty throne I bow the knee, \\
        Then of Thy love in all its breadth and length, \\
        Its height and depth, its everlasting strength, \\
            My soul shall sing.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 250}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. BELMONT}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HOW sweet the name of Jesus sounds \\
            In a believer's ear! \\
        It soothes his sorrows, heals his wounds, \\
            And drives away his fear.

    \item It makes the wounded spirit whole, \\
            And calms the troubled breast; \\
        'Tis manna to the hungry soul, \\
            And to the weary rest.

    \item Dear Name! the rock on which we build, \\
            Our shield and hiding place; \\
        Our never-failing treasury, filled \\
            With boundless stores of grace.

    \item Lord Jesus, Saviour, Shepherd, Friend, \\
            Our Prophet, Priest and King, \\
        Our Lord, our Life, our Way, our End, \\
            Accept the praise we bring.

    \item Weak is the effort of the heart, \\
            And cold our warmest thought; \\
        But when we see Thee as Thou art, \\
            We'll praise Thee as we ought.

    \item Till then we would Thy love proclaim \\
            With every fleeting breath; \\
        And glory in Thy bless\`ed name \\
            That quells the power of death.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 251}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. LOUGHTON}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THERE is a name I love to hear, \\
            I love to speak its worth; \\
        It sounds like music in mine ear, \\
            The sweetest name on earth.

    \item It tells me of a Saviour's love, \\
            Who died to set me free; \\
        It tells me of His precious blood, \\
            The sinner's only plea.

    \item It tells me of a Father's love \\
            Beaming upon His child; \\
        It cheers me through this little while, \\
            Through desert, waste and wild.

    \item It tells me what my Father hath \\
            In store for me each day; \\
        And though I tread a darksome path, \\
            Yields sunshine all the way.

    \item It tells of One whose loving heart \\
            Can feel my every woe, \\
        Who in each sorrow bears a part \\
            That none can bear below.

    \item It bids my trembling soul rejoice, \\
            It dries each rising tear; \\
        It tells me in a still small voice, \\
            To trust and not to fear.

    \item "Jesus," the name I love so well, \\
            The name I love to hear; \\
        No saint on earth its worth can tell, \\
            No heart conceive how dear.

    \item This name shall shed its fragrance still \\
            Along the thorny road, \\
        Shall sweetly smooth the rugged hill \\
            That leads me up to God;

    \item And there, with all the blood-bought throng, \\
            From sin and sorrow free, \\
        I'll sing the new, eternal song, \\
            His boundless love to me.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 252}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D MUNICH}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus, Friend unfailing, \\
            How dear Thou art to me! \\
        Are cares or fears assailing? \\
            I find my strength in Thee. \\
        Why should my feet grow weary \\
            Of this my pilgrim way? \\
        Rough though the path and dreary, \\
            It ends in perfect day.

    \item Nought, nought I court as pleasure, \\
            Compared, O Christ, with Thee. \\
        Thy sorrow without measure \\
            Earned peace and joy for me. \\
        I love to own, Lord Jesus, \\
            Thy claims o'er me divine; \\
        Bought with Thy blood most precious, \\
            Whose can I be but Thine?

    \item What fills my heart with gladness? \\
            'Tis Thine abounding grace. \\
        Where can I look in sadness, \\
            O Lord, but on Thy face? \\
        My all is Thy providing, \\
            Thy love can ne'er grow cold. \\
        In Thee, my refuge, hiding, \\
            No good wilt Thou withhold.

    \item Why should I droop in sorrow? \\
            Thou'rt ever by my side. \\
        Why, trembling, dread the morrow, \\
            What ill can e'er betide? \\
        If I my cross have taken, \\
            'Tis but to follow Thee; \\
        If scorned, despised, forsaken, \\
            Nought severs Thee from me.

    \item O worldly pomp and glory, \\
            Your charms are spread in vain. \\
        I've heard a sweeter story, \\
            I've found a truer gain. \\
        Where Christ a place prepareth, \\
            There is my loved abode, \\
        There shall I gaze upon Him, \\
            There ever dwell with God.

    \item For every tribulation, \\
            For every sore distress, \\
        In Christ I've full salvation, \\
            Sure help and quiet rest. \\
        No fear of foes prevailing, \\
            I triumph, Lord, in Thee. \\
        Lord Jesus, Friend unfailing, \\
            How dear Thou art to me!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 253}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. VAIL}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THOU, my everlasting portion, \\
            More than friend or life to me, \\
        All along my pilgrim journey, \\
            Saviour, let me walk with Thee, \\
                Close to Thee. \\
            All along my pilgrim journey, \\
            Saviour, let me walk with Thee.

    \item Not for ease or worldly pleasure, \\
            Not for fame my prayer shall be; \\
        Gladly would I toil and suffer, \\
            Only let me walk with Thee, \\
                Close to Thee. \\
            Gladly would I toil and suffer, \\
            Only let me walk with Thee.

    \item Lead me through the vale of shadows; \\
            Bear me o'er life's fitful sea; \\
        Then the gate of life eternal \\
            I shall enter, Lord, with Thee, \\
                Close to Thee. \\
            Then the gate of life eternal \\
            I shall enter, Lord, with Thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 254}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D I'VE FOUND A FRIEND}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I'VE found a Friend, oh, such a Friend! \\
            He loved me ere I knew Him; \\
        He drew me with the cords of love, \\
            And thus He bound me to Him. \\
        And round my heart still closely twine \\
            Those ties which nought can sever; \\
        For I am His and He is mine, \\
            For ever and for ever.

    \item I've found a Friend, oh, such a Friend! \\
            He bled, He died to save me; \\
        And not alone the gift of life, \\
            But His own self He gave me. \\
        Nought that I have mine own I'd call, \\
            I hold it for the Giver; \\
        My heart, my strength, my life, my all, \\
            Are His, and His for ever.

    \item I've found a Friend, oh, such a Friend! \\
            All power to Him is giv\`en, \\
        To guard me on my onward course \\
            And bring me safe to heav\`en. \\
        Th'eternal glories gleam afar \\
            To nerve my faint endeavour; \\
        So now to watch, to work, to war, \\
            And then, to rest for ever.

    \item I've found a Friend, oh, such a Friend! \\
            So kind, and true, and tender; \\
        So wise a Counsellor and Guide, \\
            So mighty a Defender! \\
        From Him, who loves me now so well, \\
            What power my soul shall sever? \\
        Shall life or death? Shall earth or hell? \\
            No, I am His for ever.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 255}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.4.4.8.8. I HAVE A FRIEND}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I HAVE a Friend, whose faithful love \\
        Is more than all the world to me; \\
        'Tis higher than the heights above, \\
        And deeper than the soundless sea. \\
            So old, so new, \\
            So strong, so true; \\
        Before the earth received its frame \\
        He loved me. Bless\`ed be His name!

    \item He held the highest place above, \\
        Adored by all the sons of flame; \\
        Yet, such His self-denying love, \\
        He laid aside His crown and came \\
            To seek the lost, \\
            And, at the cost \\
        Of heavenly rank and earthly fame, \\
        He sought me. Bless\`ed be His name!

    \item It was a lonely path He trod, \\
        From every human soul apart; \\
        Known only to Himself and God \\
        Was all the grief that filled His heart; \\
            Yet from the track \\
            He turned not back \\
        Till, where I lay in want and shame, \\
        He found me. Bless\`ed be His name!

    \item Then dawned at last that day of dread \\
        When, desolate, yet undismayed, \\
        With wearied frame and thorn-crowned head \\
        He, now forsaken and betrayed, \\
            Went up for me \\
            To Calvary, \\
        And, dying there in grief and shame, \\
        He saved me. Bless\`ed be His name!

    \item Long as I live my soul shall tell \\
        The wonders of His matchless love; \\
        And when at last I rise to dwell \\
        In the bright home prepared above, \\
            My joy shall be \\
            His face to see, \\
        And, bowing then with loud acclaim, \\
        I'll praise Him. Bless\`ed be His name!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 256}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. CLARENDON STREET}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus, I love Thee, I know Thou art mine, \\
        My rock and my fortress, my surety divine; \\
        My gracious Redeemer, my Saviour art Thou; \\
        'Tis Thou who art worthy, Lord Jesus, 'tis Thou.

    \item I love Thee because Thou hast first lov\`ed me, \\
        And purchased my pardon on Calv\`ary's tree; \\
        I love Thee for wearing the thorns on Thy brow; \\
        'Tis Thou who art worthy, Lord Jesus, 'tis Thou.

    \item I will love Thee in life, I will love Thee in death, \\
        And praise Thee as long as Thou lendest me breath, \\
        And say if the death-dew lie cold on my brow, \\
        " 'Tis Thou who art worthy, Lord Jesus, 'tis Thou."

    \item In mansions of glory and endless delight, \\
        I then will adore Thee in regions of light, \\
        And sing with the glitt\`ering crown on my brow, \\
        " 'Tis Thou who art worthy, Lord Jesus, 'tis Thou."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 257}}
    \\
    \textit{6.4.6.4. D VERONICA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item CLING to the Mighty One, \\
            Cling in thy grief; \\
        Cling to the Holy One, \\
            He gives relief; \\
        Cling to the gracious One, \\
            Cling in thy pain; \\
        Cling to the faithful One, \\
            He will sustain.

    \item Cling to the Living One, \\
            Cling in thy woe; \\
        Cling to the loving One, \\
            Through all below; \\
        Cling to the pardoning One, \\
            He speaketh peace; \\
        Cling to the healing One, \\
            Anguish shall cease.

    \item Cling to the wounded One, \\
            Cling to His side; \\
        Cling to the risen One, \\
            In Him abide; \\
        Cling to the coming One, \\
            Hope shall arise; \\
        Cling to the reigning One, \\
            Joy lights thine eyes.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 258}}
    \\
    \textit{8.5.8.3. BULLINGER}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item ART thou weary, art thou languid, \\
            Art thou sore distressed? \\
        "Come to Me," saith One, "and coming, \\
            Be at rest!"

    \item Hath He marks to lead me to Him, \\
            He who is my guide? \\
        In His feet and hands are wound prints, \\
            And His side.

    \item Hath He diadem, as monarch, \\
            That His brow adorns? \\
        Yea, a crown in very surety, \\
            But of thorns.

    \item If my Master close I follow, \\
            What His guerdon here? \\
        Many a sorrow, many a labour, \\
            Many a tear.

    \item If I still walk closely with Him, \\
            What hath He at last? \\
        Sorrow vanquished, labour ended, \\
            Jordan passed.

    \item If I ask Him to sustain me, \\
            Will He say me Nay? \\
        Not though earth and not though heaven \\
            Pass away.

    \item Finding, following, keeping, struggling--- \\
            Is He sure to bless? \\
        Saints, apostles, prophets, martyrs, \\
            Answer, "Yes."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 259}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. PRECIOUS NAME}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item TAKE the name of Jesus with you \\
            In your path of weal and woe; \\
        It will joy and comfort give you, \\
            Take it then where'er you go.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Precious name! oh, how sweet!} \\
    \textit{Hope of earth and joy of heaven.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Take the name of Jesus ever, \\
            As a shield from every snare. \\
        If temptations round you gather, \\
            Breathe that holy name in prayer.

    \item Oh, the precious name of Jesus, \\
            How it thrills our souls with joy! \\
        Let us tell of Him who guards us; \\
            Let His songs our tongues employ.

    \item At the name of Jesus bowing, \\
            Falling prostrate at His feet, \\
        King of kings in heaven we'll own Him, \\
            When our journey is complete.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 260}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8. CELESTE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WE'LL sing of the Shepherd that died, \\
        That died for the sake of the flock; \\
        His love to the utmost was tried, \\
        But firmly endured as a rock.

    \item When blood from a victim must flow, \\
        This Shepherd, by pity, was led \\
        To stand in our place and to know \\
        The suffering and death in our stead.

    \item Our song, then, for ever shall be \\
        Of the Shepherd who gave Himself thus; \\
        No subject's so glorious as He, \\
        Nor theme so affecting to us.

    \item We'd sing of such subjects alone, \\
        None other our tongues should employ, \\
        Till better His love becomes known \\
        In yonder bright regions of joy.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 261}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.8.8. ST. MATTHIAS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LORD Jesus, teach us still to keep \\
        Our eyes on Thee, the living way; \\
        That we, once lost and wandering sheep, \\
        From Thee, our Lord, no more may stray; \\
        But wheresoe'er Thou leadest, we \\
        May follow on most cheerfully.

    \item Oh, that we never might forget \\
        What Thou hast suffered for our sake, \\
        To save our souls, and make us meet \\
        In all Thy glory to partake; \\
        But keeping this in sight, press on \\
        To glory and a victor's crown!

    \item But, gracious Lord, when we reflect \\
        How oft we've turned our eye from Thee, \\
        Have treated Thee with sad neglect \\
        And listened to the enemy; \\
        And yet to find Thee still the same--- \\
        'Tis this that humbles us with shame.

    \item Astonished at Thy feet we fall; \\
        Thy love exceeds our highest thought. \\
        Henceforth be Thou our all in all, \\
        Thou, who our souls with blood hast bought. \\
        May we henceforth more faithful prove, \\
        And ne'er forget Thy ceaseless love.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 262}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. CRIMOND}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THE Lord's my Shepherd, I'll not want; \\
            He makes me down to lie \\
        In pastures green; He leadeth me \\
            The quiet waters by.

    \item My soul He doth restore again; \\
            And me to walk doth make \\
        Within the paths of righteousness, \\
            E'en for His own name's sake.

    \item Yea, though I walk in death's dark vale, \\
            Yet will I fear none ill; \\
        For Thou art with me, and Thy rod \\
            And staff me comfort still.

    \item My table Thou hast furnish\`ed \\
            In presence of my foes; \\
        My head Thou dost with oil anoint, \\
            And my cup overflows.

    \item Goodness and mercy all my life \\
            Shall surely follow me; \\
        And in God's house for evermore \\
            My dwelling place shall be.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 263}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. DOMINUS REGIT ME}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THE King of love my Shepherd is, \\
            Whose goodness faileth never; \\
        I nothing lack since I am His \\
            And He is mine for ever.

    \item Where streams of living water flow \\
            My ransomed soul He leadeth; \\
        And where the verdant pastures grow, \\
            With food celestial feedeth.

    \item Perverse and foolish oft I strayed, \\
            But yet in love He sought me, \\
        And on His shoulder gently laid \\
            And home rejoicing brought me.

    \item In death's dark vale I fear no ill \\
            With Thee, dear Lord, beside me; \\
        Thy rod and staff my comfort still, \\
            Thy cross before to guide me.

    \item And so through all the length of days \\
            Thy goodness faileth never; \\
        Good Shepherd, may I sing Thy praise \\
            Within God's house for ever.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 264}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. PROVIDENCE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JEHOVAH'S my Shepherd, no want shall I know, \\
        He maketh me lie down where green pastures grow; \\
        Beside the still waters He tenderly guides; \\
        My soul He restoreth by things He provides.

    \item He leadeth me then, for the sake of His name, \\
        In paths that are righteous, to walk in the same; \\
        E'en though through the valley, death's shadow, I tread, \\
        Because Thou art with me no ill will I dread.

    \item Thou comfortest me by Thy rod and Thy staff; \\
        With enemies nigh me, Thou, on my behalf, \\
        Preparest a table. Then doest Thou more--- \\
        My head Thou anointest; my cup runneth o'er.

    \item I've nought, all the days of my life, to repine, \\
        Since goodness and mercy shall surely be mine; \\
        The house of Jehovah my dwelling shall be \\
        Both now and for ever. Praise be unto Thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 265}}
    \\
    \textit{C. M. D. RECOMPENSE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item OUR Lord His glory laid aside, \\
            That He had known with God, \\
        Then came to earth as man and died \\
            To cleanse us by His blood. \\
        Good Shepherd He, stray sheep He sought, \\
            Stooped low and suffered loss; \\
        He bore the hiding of God's face, \\
            A curse upon the cross.

    \item He's risen now, and lives on high, \\
            Again He's glorified; \\
        And ever liveth there to save \\
            His sheep for whom He died. \\
        As Shepherd Great, with crook in hand, \\
            He tends them all with care; \\
        E'en through death's shadow, and midst foes, \\
            He's present with them there.

    \item Soon, as Chief Shepherd, He will come \\
            His loved ones home to take, \\
        When those who've served shall have reward, \\
            Who wrought for His name's sake. \\
        Crowned King of glory then He'll be, \\
            And Lord of Hosts beside; \\
        For Him the heads of lasting doors, \\
            And gates will open wide.

    \item The Shepherd thus, Good, Great, and Chief, \\
            By cross, and crook, and crown, \\
        Salvation threefold will have wrought \\
            For those He calls His own. \\
        Our God, we're longing for that day \\
            To see the Shepherd King, \\
        Who, with all gathered saints at home, \\
            Will lead the song they sing.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 266}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. GOSHEN}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JESUS is our Shepherd, wiping every tear; \\
        Folded in His bosom, what have we to fear? \\
        Only let us follow whither He doth lead, \\
        To the thirsty desert or the dewy mead.

    \item Jesus is our Shepherd, well we know His voice; \\
        How its gentlest whisper makes our heart rejoice! \\
        Even when He chideth, tender is His tone; \\
        None but He shall guide us. We are His alone.

    \item Jesus is our Shepherd, for His sheep He bled; \\
        Every lamb is sprinkled with the blood He shed; \\
        Then on each He setteth His own secret sign, \\
        "They that have My Spirit, these," saith He, "are Mine."

    \item Jesus is our Shepherd, guarded by His arm, \\
        Though the wolves may ravin, none can do us harm. \\
        Should we tread death's valley, dark with fearful gloom, \\
        We will fear no evil, victors o'er the tomb.

    \item Jesus is our Shepherd, with His goodness now \\
        And His tender mercy, He doth us endow; \\
        Let us sing His praises with a gladsome heart, \\
        Very soon we'll meet Him, never more to part.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\thispagestyle{empty}
\vspace*{3.5cm}
\begin{center}
    {\LARGE \textbf{SECTION 11}}\\[0.8em]
    {\Large \textsc{Praise to, and Communion with the Lord Jesus Christ}}
\end{center}
\vspace{3.5cm}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 267}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. SOLYMA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O PATIENT, spotless One, \\
        Our hearts in meekness train \\
        To bear Thy yoke and learn of Thee, \\
        That we may rest obtain.

    \item O Lord, Thou art enough \\
        The mind and heart to fill; \\
        Thy life to calm each anxious thought, \\
        Thy love each fear dispel.

    \item Oh, fix our earnest gaze \\
        So wholly, Lord, on Thee \\
        That, with Thy beauty occupied, \\
        We elsewhere none may see. Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 268}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M. CLAREMONT}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item OH, teach us more of Thy blest ways, \\
        Thou holy Lamb of God; \\
        And fix and root us in Thy grace, \\
        As those redeemed by blood.

    \item Oh, tell us often of Thy love, \\
        Of all Thy grief and pain; \\
        And let our hearts with joy confess \\
        That thence comes all our gain.

    \item For this, oh, may we freely count \\
        Whate'er we have but loss; \\
        The dearest object of our love, \\
        Compared with Thee but dross!

    \item Engrave this deeply on our heart \\
        With an eternal pen, \\
        That we may, in some small degree, \\
        Return Thy love again. Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 269}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. SARRATT}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item OH come, Thou stricken Lamb of God, \\
        Who shedd'st for us Thine own life-blood, \\
        And teach us all Thy love; then pain \\
        Were sweet, and life or death were gain.

    \item Take Thou our hearts, and let them be \\
        For ever closed to all but Thee; \\
        Thy willing servants, let us wear \\
        The seal of love for ever there.

    \item How blest are they who still abide \\
        Close sheltered by Thy watchful side, \\
        Who life and strength from Thee receive, \\
        And with Thee move, and in Thee live!

    \item How can it be, Thou heavenly King, \\
        That Thou shouldst man to glory bring, \\
        Make slaves the partners of Thy throne, \\
        Decked with a never-fading crown?

    \item O Lord, enlarge our scanty thought \\
        To know the wonders Thou hast wrought; \\
        Unloose our stammering tongues to tell \\
        Thy love immense, unsearchable.

    \item First-born of many brethren Thou, \\
        To whom both heaven and earth must bow! \\
        Heirs of Thy shame and of Thy throne, \\
        We bear the cross and seek the crown.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 270}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.6. JUST AS I AM}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O HOLY Saviour, Friend unseen, \\
        Since on Thine arm Thou bidd'st us lean, \\
        Help us throughout life's changing scene \\
        By faith to cling to Thee.

    \item Blest with this fellowship divine, \\
        Take what Thou wilt, we'll ne'er repine; \\
        Since, blessed Saviour, we are Thine, \\
        And we may cling to Thee.

    \item Far from our home, fatigued, oppressed, \\
        Here we have found our place of rest, \\
        As exiles still, yet not unblest, \\
        While we can cling to Thee.

    \item Without a murmur, we'd dismiss \\
        Our former dreams of earthly bliss; \\
        Our joy, our consolation this--- \\
        Each hour to cling to Thee.

    \item What though the world deceitful prove, \\
        And earthly friends and hopes remove? \\
        With patient, uncomplaining love, \\
        Still would we cling to Thee.

    \item Oft when we seem to tread alone \\
        Some barren waste with thorns o'ergrown, \\
        Thy voice of love in gentlest tone \\
        Whispers, ``Still cling to Me.''

    \item Blest is our lot, whate'er befall; \\
        What can affright or who appal, \\
        While as our strength, our rock, our all, \\
        Saviour, we cling to Thee?

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 271}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M. FARRANT}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O CHILD of God, there is for thee \\
        One only safe retreat--- \\
        Where Jesus is thy heart should be, \\
        Thy dwelling at His feet.

    \item When Satan tracks thy lonely way, \\
        There his temptations meet; \\
        In Jesus' presence watch and pray, \\
        Yea, conquer at His feet.

    \item Since thou hast much to learn, although \\
        Thou art in Christ complete, \\
        In grace and knowledge seek to grow, \\
        By sitting at His feet.

    \item Through tribulation hasten on, \\
        With Christ the cross is sweet; \\
        The little while will soon be gone; \\
        Keep only at His feet.

    \item O child of God, forget the past, \\
        Prepare thy Lord to greet, \\
        That thou may'st share His throne, and cast \\
        Thy crown before His feet.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 272}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M. EVANGELIST}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LORD, I would delight in Thee, \\
        And on Thy care depend; \\
        To Thee in every trouble flee, \\
        My sure, my steadfast Friend.

    \item When human cisterns all are dried, \\
        Thy fulness is the same; \\
        May I with this be satisfied, \\
        And glory in Thy name.

    \item Why should I thirst for aught below, \\
        While there's a fountain near, \\
        A fountain which doth ever flow, \\
        The fainting heart to cheer?

    \item No good in creatures can be found \\
        Apart, O Lord, from Thee. \\
        I must have all things and abound \\
        Since Thou art all to me.

    \item Oh, that I had but simpler faith \\
        To live within the veil; \\
        To feed on what the Lord hath said, \\
        Whose word can never fail!

    \item He that hath made my heaven secure, \\
        Will all I need provide. \\
        While Christ is rich, can I be poor--- \\
        Christ, who for me hath died?

    \item O Lord, I cast my care on Thee; \\
        I triumph and adore; \\
        Oh, that my great concern may be \\
        To love and praise Thee more!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 273}}
    \\
    \textit{10.10.10.10. EVENTIDE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item ABIDE with me; fast falls the eventide; \\
        The darkness deepens, Lord, with me abide. \\
        When other helpers fail, and comforts flee, \\
        Help of the helpless, oh abide with me!

    \item Swift to its close ebbs out life's little day; \\
        Earth's joys grow dim, its glories pass away; \\
        Change and decay in all around I see; \\
        O Thou who changest not, abide with me!

    \item Not a brief glance I beg, a passing word; \\
        But as Thou dwelt'st with Thy disciples, Lord, \\
        Familiar, condescending, patient, free, \\
        Come not to sojourn, but abide with me!

    \item Thou on my head in early youth didst smile, \\
        And, though rebellious and perverse meanwhile, \\
        Thou hast not left me, oft as I left Thee; \\
        On to the close, O Lord, abide with me!

    \item I need Thy presence every passing hour; \\
        What but Thy grace can foil the tempter's power? \\
        Who like Thyself my guide and stay can be? \\
        Through cloud and sunshine, oh abide with me!

    \item I fear no foe, with Thee at hand to bless; \\
        Ills have no weight, and tears no bitterness. \\
        Where is death's sting, and where death's vict\`ory? \\
        I triumph still if Thou abide with me.

    \item Hold Thou Thy cross before my faltering eyes, \\
        Shine through the gloom, and point me to the skies. \\
        Morn soon shall break, and earth's vain shadows flee: \\
        Till that bright dawning, Lord, abide with me.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 274}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D DOANE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LAMB of God, still keep me \\
        Near to Thy wounded side; \\
        'Tis only there in safety \\
        And peace I can abide. \\
        What foes and snares surround me! \\
        What lusts and fears within! \\
        The grace that sought and found me \\
        Alone can keep me clean.

    \item 'Tis only in Thee hiding, \\
        I know my life secure; \\
        Only in Thee abiding, \\
        The conflict can endure. \\
        Thine arm the victory gaineth \\
        O'er every hurtful foe; \\
        Thy love my heart sustaineth \\
        In all its cares and woe.

    \item Soon shall my eyes behold Thee \\
        With rapture face to face; \\
        One half hath not been told me \\
        Of all Thy power and grace. \\
        Thy beauty, Lord, and glory, \\
        The wonders of Thy love, \\
        Shall be the endless story \\
        Of all Thy saints above.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 275}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular THE CROSS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item BENEATH Thy cross, Lord Jesus, \\
        I fain would take my stand--- \\
        The shadow of a mighty rock \\
        Within a weary land; \\
        A home within the wilderness, \\
        A rest upon the way, \\
        From the burning of the noon-tide heat \\
        And the burden of the day.

    \item O safe and happy shelter, \\
        O refuge tried and sweet, \\
        O trysting-place, where heav\`en's love \\
        And heav\`en's justice meet! \\
        As to the holy patriarch \\
        That wondrous dream was given, \\
        So seems my Saviour's cross to me \\
        A ladder up to heaven.

    \item There lies beneath its shadow, \\
        But on the farther side, \\
        The darkness of an awful grave \\
        That gapes both deep and wide; \\
        And there between us stands the cross, \\
        Two arms outstretched to save, \\
        Like a watchman set to guard the way \\
        From that eternal grave.

    \item Upon that cross of Jesus \\
        My eyes at times can see \\
        The very dying form of One \\
        Who suffered there for me; \\
