        Lord, we long to see Thee come.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 340}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7.4.7. SAVIOUR, LEAD US}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate. The last two lines to be repeated.]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SAVIOUR, through the desert lead us, \\
        Without Thee we cannot go; \\
        Thou from day to day must feed us, \\
        Thou canst conquer every foe; \\
        Drink and food are in Thy hand, \\
        Lead us to the promised land.

    \item Through a world of endless dangers \\
        Do we journey day by day; \\
        Strangers to the world, and pilgrims, \\
        Keep us in the narrow way; \\
        Keep us by Thy mighty hand, \\
        Lead us to the promised land.

    \item When we pass the swelling river, \\
        Jordan's cold, tumultuous tide, \\
        Deliver us from fear for ever, \\
        Bear us safe to Canaan's side; \\
        Place us with the ransomed band, \\
        In that bless\`ed, glorious land.

    \item There, in unclouded glory shining, \\
        Jesus, we shall see Thy face; \\
        With the angels' anthems joining, \\
        Sing the triumphs of Thy grace; \\
        Resting on that happy strand, \\
        Safe in our eternal land.

    \item When we hunger Thou wilt feed us, \\
        Manna shall our camp surround; \\
        Faint and thirsty Thou wilt heed us, \\
        Streams shall from the rock abound. \\
        Happy people! \\
        What a Saviour we have found!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 341}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D LUX EOI}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item RISE, my soul, thy God directs thee, \\
        Stranger hands no more impede; \\
        Pass thou on, His hand protects thee, \\
        Strength that has the captive freed. \\
        Is the wilderness before thee, \\
        Desert lands where drought abounds? \\
        Heavenly springs shall there restore thee, \\
        Fresh from God's exhaustless tides.

    \item Light divine surrounds thy going, \\
        God Himself shall mark thy way; \\
        Secret blessings richly flowing \\
        Lead to everlasting day. \\
        God, thine everlasting portion, \\
        Feeds thee with the mighty's meat; \\
        Price of Egypt's hard extortion, \\
        Egypt's food, no more to eat.

    \item Art thou weaned from Egypt's pleasures? \\
        God in secret thee shall keep; \\
        There unfold His hidden treasures, \\
        There, His love's exhaustless deep. \\
        In the desert God will teach thee \\
        What the God that thou hast found; \\
        Patient, gracious, powerful, holy; \\
        All His grace shall there abound.

    \item On to Canaan's rest still wending, \\
        E'en thy wants and woes shall bring \\
        Suited grace from high descending; \\
        Thou shalt taste of mercy's spring. \\
        Though thy way be long and dreary, \\
        Eagle strength He'll still renew; \\
        Garments fresh and foot unweary, \\
        Tell how God has brought thee through.

    \item When to Canaan's long-loved dwelling \\
        Love divine thy foot shall bring, \\
        There with shouts of triumph swelling \\
        Zion's songs in rest to sing, \\
        There no stranger-God shall meet thee \\
        (Stranger thou in courts above); \\
        He who to His rest shall greet thee \\
        Greets thee with a well-known love.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 342}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. STEINER}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I'M a pilgrim bound for glory, \\
        I'm a pilgrim going home; \\
        Come and hear me tell my story, \\
        All who love the Saviour, come.

    \item I will tell you what induced me \\
        For the better land to start--- \\
        'Twas the Saviour's loving-kindness \\
        Overcame and won my heart.

    \item Faint and weary He has brought me \\
        To the fountain of His love; \\
        Showed me how His blood had bought me, \\
        Sealed my pardon from above.

    \item Through the wilderness He's led me, \\
        Strength in weakness He's bestowed; \\
        With the bread of life He's fed me, \\
        Streams of living water flowed.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 343}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. MARCHING}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FROM the cross unto the glory, \\
        Through the darksome vale of tears; \\
        Trusting, serving and obeying, \\
        It may be for days or years.

    \item From the cross unto the glory, \\
        With the Saviour nigh at hand; \\
        Sooner, later, all arriving \\
        In the pleasant promised land.

    \item From the cross unto the glory, \\
        With a happy pilgrim band; \\
        Bright or cloudy be the pathway, \\
        By His grace we soon shall land.

    \item To the cross down from the glory, \\
        First He came to set us free; \\
        Soon He comes, and raised in glory \\
        We shall ever with Him be.

    \item From the cross unto the glory, \\
        Him as Lord I gladly own; \\
        Telling all the wondrous story \\
        Of the One who wears the crown.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 344}}
    \\
    \textit{7.5.7.5. D HIDING IN THEE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THROUGH the dark path of sorrow \\
        Which Jesus has trod, \\
        Thy feeble ones journey, \\
        O Father, our God. \\
        And the thick clouds that gather \\
        But turn us away \\
        From the waste, howling desert, \\
        Where He could not stay.

    \item From the waste, howling desert, \\
        Where He could not stay; \\
        Thy hand leadeth onward, \\
        Thy counsel and light \\
        Guide through the dark shadow \\
        Of this dreary night. \\
        In the desert He met us, \\
        In mercy and grace; \\
        And gently He drew us \\
        To seek Thy blest face.

    \item And the rough road of sorrow, \\
        Which once made us sigh, \\
        Now brings to our spirits \\
        A peace from on high. \\
        For the foot-prints of Jesus \\
        Are marked on the road, \\
        And lead us in safety \\
        To Thine own abode.

    \item And the bright home in glory, \\
        Whose light shines so clear, \\
        Dispels every shadow, \\
        And dries every tear. \\
        With the loved One before us, \\
        Who died for us there, \\
        His likeness for ever \\
        We gladly shall bear.

    \item There, in unclouded brightness, \\
        Thy face we shall see, \\
        And praise Thee for ever, \\
        Our Father, in Thee. \\
        From the waste, howling desert, \\
        Thy children are come, \\
        To rest in the haven \\
        Of Thine own blest home.

\end{enumerate}
\vspace{1em}
\noindent \textit{The last syllable of verse 3, line 7, and of verse 4, line 3, are to be sung to the first note of the following line.}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 345}}
    \\
    \textit{6.4.6.4.6.6.6.4. FATHERLAND}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I'M but a stranger here, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        Earth is a desert drear, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        Danger and sorrow stand \\
        Round me on every hand; \\
        Heaven is my fatherland, \\
        Heaven is my home.

    \item What though the tempest rage, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        Short is my pilgrimage, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        And time's wild wintry blast \\
        Soon shall be overpast; \\
        I shall reach home at last, \\
        Heaven is my home.

    \item There at my Saviour's side, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        I shall be glorified, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        There are the good and blest, \\
        Those I loved most and best; \\
        And there I too shall rest, \\
        Heaven is my home.

    \item Therefore I murmur not, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        Whate'er my earthly lot, \\
        Heaven is my home; \\
        And I shall surely stand \\
        There at my Lord's right hand; \\
        Heaven is my fatherland, \\
        Heaven is my home.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 346}}
    \\
    \textit{8.4.8.4.8.4. WENTWORTH}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item MY God, I thank Thee, Who hast made \\
        The earth so bright; \\
        So full of splendour and of joy, \\
        Beauty and light; \\
        So many glorious things are here, \\
        Noble and right.

    \item I thank Thee, too, that Thou hast made \\
        Joy to abound; \\
        So many gentle thoughts and deeds \\
        Circling us round, \\
        That in the darkest spot of earth \\
        Some love is found.

    \item I thank Thee more that all our joy \\
        Is touched with pain; \\
        That shadows fall on earthly light, \\
        That flowers take rain; \\
        That something in our brightest days \\
        We still must gain.

    \item For Thou wouldst have us look above \\
        For full increase; \\
        Not linger in the path of time, \\
        Nor take our ease, \\
        Until we reach that heavenly rest \\
        Where struggles cease.

    \item I thank Thee, Lord, that Thou hast kept \\
        The best in store; \\
        We have enough, yet not too much \\
        To long for more: \\
        A yearning for a deeper peace \\
        Not known before.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 347}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.13. HASTEN}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item RISE up and hasten! my soul, haste along, \\
        And speed on thy journey with hope and with song; \\
        Home, home is nearing, 'tis nearing each day, \\
        Then tarry no longer, nor faint by the way.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Home, home is nearing, 'tis nearing each day,} \\
    \textit{Then tarry no longer, nor faint by the way.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Why shouldst thou linger? what here canst thou gain? \\
        A world where all's fleeting, all's empty and vain; \\
        Where grief and sorrow and trouble are rife, \\
        A pathway of sorrow, of conflict and strife.

    \item Rise up and hasten! with joy in thy breast, \\
        For this is not, cannot be, shall not be thy rest; \\
        There's rest in the presence of Jesus our Lord, \\
        According to His most sure, covenant word.

    \item Look forward, my soul, to that bright, bless\`ed day, \\
        When tears shall be wiped from all faces away; \\
        When the Bridegroom shall come for His loved and His own, \\
        To share in His kingdom, His glory, His throne.

    \item Then courage, my soul! though the pathway be drear, \\
        Thy Saviour is with thee, thy Lord is still near; \\
        Soon, soon shalt thou see Him, and fall at His feet, \\
        Where the praise and the worship shall then be complete.

\end{enumerate}
\vspace{1em}
\noindent \textit{The notes in small type are required for the first syllable in each of the following lines:---line 2 of verse 1; line 3 of verse 2; line 4 of every verse.}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 348}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7.8.8.7. WHITHER, PILGRIMS?}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate. The sixth line to be repeated.]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item "WHITHER, pilgrims, are you going, \\
        Going each with staff in hand?" \\
        We are going on a journey, \\
        Going at our King's command. \\
        Over mountain, plain, and stream, \\
        We are going to the palace of the King, \\
        Going to the palace of the King.

    \item "Tell us, pilgrims, what you hope for \\
        In that far-off, better land?" \\
        Spotless robes and crowns of glory \\
        From a Saviour's loving hand. \\
        We shall drink of life's clear stream, \\
        We shall dwell with the King for evermore, \\
        Dwell with the King for evermore.

    \item "Pilgrims, may we travel with you \\
        To that bright and happy home?" \\
        Jesus calls you; come and welcome; \\
        Start to-day; no longer roam. \\
        Come with us and seek the grace, \\
        Come and share in the blessing of the King, \\
        Share in the blessing of the King.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 349}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7. DA CHRISTUS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item CHILDREN of the heavenly King, \\
        As ye journey, sweetly sing; \\
        Sing your Saviour's worthy praise, \\
        Glorious in His works and ways.

    \item We are travelling home to God, \\
        In the way the fathers trod; \\
        They are happy now, and we \\
        Soon their happiness shall see.

    \item Shout, ye little flock, and blest, \\
        You on Jesus' throne shall rest; \\
        There your seat is now prepared, \\
        There your kingdom and reward.

    \item Fear not, brethren, joyful stand \\
        On the borders of your land; \\
        Jesus Christ, your Father's Son, \\
        Bids you undismayed go on.

    \item Lord! obediently we'll go, \\
        Gladly leaving all below; \\
        Only Thou our Leader be, \\
        And we still will follow Thee.

    \item Hymns of glory, songs of praise, \\
        Father, unto Thee we raise; \\
        Praise to Thee, O Christ, our King, \\
        And the Holy Ghost we sing.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 350}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7. DENT DALE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast [Arranged and harmonized by R. Vaughan Williams]]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item OFT in danger, oft in woe, \\
        Onward, Christians, onward go; \\
        Fight the fight, maintain the strife, \\
        Strengthened with the bread of life.

    \item Let your drooping hearts be glad; \\
        March, in heavenly armour clad; \\
        Fight, nor think the battle long; \\
        Soon shall victory tune your song.

    \item Let not sorrow dim your eye, \\
        Soon shall every tear be dry; \\
        Let not fears your course impede, \\
        Great your strength, if great your need.

    \item Onward then to battle move; \\
        More than conquerors ye shall prove; \\
        Though opposed by many a foe, \\
        Christian soldiers, onward go.

    \item Hymns of glory and of praise, \\
        Father, unto Thee we raise; \\
        Praise to Thee, O Christ, our King, \\
        And the Holy Ghost we sing.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 351}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.5. D MY FATHER KNOWS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I HAVE a Father in the promised land, \\
        My Father calls me, I must go \\
        To meet Him in the promised land. \\
        I'll away, I'll away to the promised land, \\
        My Father calls me, I must go \\
        To meet Him in the promised land. \\
        There'll be no more sorrow there, \\
        In that bright world above.

    \item I have a Saviour in the promised land, \\
        My Saviour calls me, I must go \\
        To meet Him in the promised land. \\
        I'll away, I'll away to the promised land, \\
        My Saviour calls me, I must go \\
        To meet Him in the promised land. \\
        There'll be no more parting there, \\
        In that bright world above.

    \item I have a crown in the promised land, \\
        My Saviour gives it, I must go \\
        To wear it in the promised land. \\
        I'll away, I'll away to the promised land, \\
        My Saviour gives it, I must go \\
        To wear it in the promised land. \\
        There'll be no more dying there, \\
        In that bright world above.

    \item I hope to meet you in the promised land, \\
        Where Jesus reigneth in His love, \\
        All glorious in the promised land. \\
        I'll away, I'll away to the promised land, \\
        Where Jesus reigneth in His love, \\
        All glorious in the promised land. \\
        We'll sing Hallelujah there, \\
        In that bright world above.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 352}}
    \\
    \textit{10.10.10.10. NAVARRE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item NOT what these hands have done \\
        Can save this guilty soul; \\
        Not what this toiling flesh has borne \\
        Can make my spirit whole.

    \item Not what I feel or do \\
        Can give me peace with God; \\
        Not all my prayers and sighs and tears \\
        Can bear this heavy load.

    \item Thy work alone, O Christ, \\
        Can ease this weight of sin; \\
        Thy blood alone, O Lamb of God, \\
        Can give me peace within.

    \item Thy love to me, O God, \\
        Not mine, O Lord, to Thee, \\
        Can rid me of this dark unrest, \\
        And set my spirit free.

    \item Thy grace alone, O God, \\
        To me can pardon speak; \\
        Thy power alone, O Son of God, \\
        Can this sore bondage break.

    \item I bless the Christ of God; \\
        I rest on love divine; \\
        And with unfaltering lip and heart \\
        I call this Saviour mine.

    \item I praise the God of grace; \\
        I trust His truth and might; \\
        He calls me His, I call Him mine, \\
        My God, my joy, my light.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 353}}
    \\
    \textit{6.6.6.6.6.6. OLD 120TH.}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THY way, not mine, O Lord, \\
        However dark it be; \\
        Lead me by Thine own hand, \\
        Choose out the path for me: \\
        Smooth let it be or rough, \\
        It will be still the best.

    \item I dare not choose my lot, \\
        I would not if I might; \\
        Choose Thou for me, my God, \\
        So shall I walk aright: \\
        The kingdom that I seek \\
        Is Thine, so let the way.

    \item Take Thou my cup, and it \\
        With joy or sorrow fill, \\
        As best to Thee may seem; \\
        Choose Thou my good and ill: \\
        Not mine, not mine the choice, \\
        In things or great or small.

    \item Be Thou my Guide, my Strength, \\
        My Wisdom and my All; \\
        And let my will be lost \\
        In Thine, my Lord, my God: \\
        So shall I safely tread \\
        The path my Saviour trod.

    \item Choose Thou for me my friends, \\
        My sickness or my health; \\
        Choose Thou my cares for me, \\
        My poverty or wealth: \\
        Not mine the choice, but Thine, \\
        To Thee I yield my all.

    \item Thy way, not mine, O Lord, \\
        However dark it be; \\
        Lead me by Thine own hand, \\
        Choose out the path for me: \\
        So shall I surely reach \\
        Thy heavenly home of rest.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 354}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7. D MAIDSTONE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item PLEASANT are Thy courts above, \\
        In the land of light and love; \\
        Pleasant are Thy courts below, \\
        In this land of sin and woe. \\
        Oh, my spirit longs and faints \\
        For the converse of Thy saints, \\
        For the brightness of Thy face, \\
        For Thy fulness, God of grace!

    \item Happy birds that sing and fly \\
        Round Thy altars, O Most High! \\
        Happier souls that find a rest \\
        In a heavenly Father's breast! \\
        Like the wandering dove, that found \\
        No repose on earth around, \\
        They can to their ark repair, \\
        And enjoy it ever there.

    \item Happy souls! their praises flow \\
        Even in this vale of woe; \\
        Waters in the desert rise, \\
        Manna feeds them from the skies; \\
        On they go from strength to strength, \\
        Till they reach Thy throne at length; \\
        At Thy feet adoring fall, \\
        Who hast led them safe through all.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 355}}
    \\
    \textit{10.7.10.7. DRAW ME NEARER}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I AM Thine, O Lord, I have heard Thy voice, \\
        And it told Thy love to me; \\
        But I long to rise in the arms of faith, \\
        And be closer drawn to Thee.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Draw me nearer, nearer, bless\`ed Lord,} \\
    \textit{To the cross where Thou hast died;} \\
    \textit{Draw me nearer, nearer, nearer, bless\`ed Lord,} \\
    \textit{To Thy precious, bleeding side.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Consecrate me now to Thy service, Lord, \\
        By the power of grace divine; \\
        Let my soul look up with a steadfast hope, \\
        And my will be lost in Thine.

    \item Oh, the pure delight of a single hour \\
        That before Thy throne I spend, \\
        When I kneel in prayer, and with Thee, my God, \\
        I commune as friend with friend!

    \item There are depths of love that I cannot know \\
        Till I cross the narrow sea; \\
        There are heights of joy that I may not reach \\
        Till I rest in peace with Thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 356}}
    \\
    \textit{8.6.8.6.8.6. ARABIA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FATHER, I know that all my life \\
        Is portioned out for me, \\
        And the changes that are sure to come \\
        I do not fear to see; \\
        But I ask Thee for a present mind \\
        Intent on pleasing Thee.

    \item I ask Thee for a thoughtful love, \\
        Through constant watching wise, \\
        To meet the glad with joyful smiles, \\
        And to wipe the weeping eyes; \\
        And a heart at leisure from itself, \\
        To soothe and sympathise.

    \item I would not have the restless will \\
        That hurries to and fro, \\
        Seeking for some great thing to do, \\
        Or secret thing to know; \\
        I would be treated as a child, \\
        And guided where I go.

    \item Wherever in the world I am, \\
        In whatsoe'er estate, \\
        I have a fellowship with hearts \\
        To keep and cultivate; \\
        And a work of lowly love to do \\
        For the Lord on Whom I wait.

    \item I ask Thee for the daily strength, \\
        To none that ask denied, \\
        And a mind to blend with outward life \\
        While keeping at Thy side; \\
        Content to fill a little space, \\
        If Thou be glorified.

    \item And if some things I do not ask \\
        In my cup of blessing be, \\
        I would have my spirit filled the more \\
        With grateful love to Thee, \\
        More careful, not to serve Thee much, \\
        But to please Thee perfectly.

    \item Briars beset our every path, \\
        Which call for patient care; \\
        There is a cross in every lot, \\
        And an earnest need for prayer; \\
        But a lowly heart that leans on Thee \\
        Is happy anywhere.

    \item In a service which Thy will appoints \\
        There are no bonds for me; \\
        For my inmost heart is taught "the truth" \\
        That makes Thy children "free;" \\
        And a life of self-renouncing love \\
        Is a life of liberty.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 357}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. TAKE TIME TO BE HOLY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item TAKE time to be holy, speak oft with thy Lord; \\
        Abide in Him always, and feed on His word; \\
        Make friends of God's children, help those who are weak, \\
        Forgetting in nothing His blessing to seek.

    \item Take time to be holy, the world rushes on; \\
        Spend much time in secret with Jesus alone; \\
        By looking to Jesus, like Him thou shalt be; \\
        Thy friends in thy conduct His likeness shall see.

    \item Take time to be holy, let Him be thy Guide, \\
        And run not before Him, whatever betide; \\
        In joy or in sorrow, still follow thy Lord, \\
        And, looking to Jesus, still trust in His word.

    \item Take time to be holy, be calm in thy soul, \\
        Each thought and each motive beneath His control; \\
        Thus led by His Spirit to fountains of love, \\
        Thou soon shalt be fitted for service above.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 358}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.4. ALMSGIVING}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item MY God, my Father, while I stray \\
        Far from my home, in life's rough way, \\
        Oh, teach me from my heart to say, \\
        "Thy will be done!"

    \item Though dark my path and sad my lot, \\
        Let me "be still" and murmur not, \\
        Or breathe the prayer divinely taught, \\
        "Thy will be done!"

    \item If Thou shouldst call me to resign \\
        What most I prize---it ne'er was mine; \\
        I only yield Thee what was Thine: \\
        "Thy will be done!"

    \item Renew my will from day to day, \\
        Blend it with Thine, and take away \\
        All that now makes it hard to say, \\
        "Thy will be done!"

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 359}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. HESPERUS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LOVE of God, how strong and true! \\
        Eternal and yet ever new; \\
        Uncomprehended and unbought, \\
        Beyond all knowledge and all thought.

    \item O wide-embracing, wondrous love, \\
        We read thee in the sky above; \\
        We read thee in the earth below, \\
        In seas that swell and streams that flow.

    \item We read thee best in Him who came \\
        To bear for us the cross of shame, \\
        Sent by the Father from on high, \\
        Our life to live, our death to die.

    \item We read thy power to bless and save \\
        E'en in the darkness of the grave; \\
        Still more in resurrection light \\
        We read the fulness of thy might.

    \item O love of God, our shield and stay \\
        Through all the perils of our way; \\
        Eternal love, in thee we rest, \\
        For ever safe, for ever blest.

    \item We read thee in the flowers that bloom, \\
        We read thee in the darksome tomb; \\
        We read thee in the starry skies, \\
        We read thee where the Saviour dies.

    \item O heavenly love, how deep and broad! \\
        The love of our unchanging God; \\
        We praise Thee now, and yet shall raise \\
        Through endless years our songs of praise.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 360}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. I SHALL BE SATISFIED}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SOUL, then know thy full salvation, \\
        Rise o'er sin and fear and care; \\
        Joy to find in every station \\
        Something still to do or bear.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{I shall be satisfied, I shall be satisfied,} \\
    \textit{When I awake in His likeness;} \\
    \textit{I shall be satisfied, I shall be satisfied,} \\
    \textit{When I awake in His likeness.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Think what Spirit dwells within thee, \\
        What a Father's smile is thine, \\
        What a Saviour died to win thee; \\
        Child of heaven, shouldst thou repine?

    \item Haste then on from grace to glory, \\
        Armed by faith and winged by prayer; \\
        Heaven's eternal day's before thee, \\
        God's own hand shall guide thee there.

    \item Soon shall close thy earthly mission, \\
        Swift shall pass thy pilgrim days; \\
        Hope soon change to glad fruition, \\
        Faith to sight, and prayer to praise.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 361}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D DAY OF REST}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O JESUS, I have promised \\
        To serve Thee to the end; \\
        Be Thou for ever near me, \\
        My Master and my Friend; \\
        I shall not fear the battle \\
        If Thou art by my side, \\
        Nor wander from the pathway \\
        If Thou wilt be my Guide.

    \item Oh, let me feel Thee near me: \\
        The world is ever near; \\
        I see the sights that dazzle, \\
        The tempting sounds I hear; \\
        My foes are ever near me, \\
        Around me and within; \\
        But, Jesus, draw Thou nearer, \\
        And shield my soul from sin.

    \item Oh, let me hear Thee speaking \\
        In accents clear and still, \\
        Above the storms of passion, \\
        The murmurs of self-will; \\
        Oh, speak to reassure me, \\
        To hasten or control; \\
        Oh, speak, and make me listen, \\
        Thou Guardian of my soul!

    \item O Jesus, Thou hast promised \\
        To all who follow Thee, \\
        That where Thou art in glory \\
        There shall Thy servant be; \\
        And, Jesus, I have promised \\
        To serve Thee to the end; \\
        Oh, give me grace to follow, \\
        My Master and my Friend!

    \item Oh, let me see Thy foot-marks, \\
        And in them plant mine own: \\
        My hope to follow duly \\
        Is in Thy strength alone. \\
        Oh, guide me, call me, draw me, \\
        Uphold me to the end; \\
        And then in heaven receive me, \\
        My Saviour and my Friend!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 362}}
    \\
    \textit{11.10.11.10. NOT NOW}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item NOT now, my child! a little more rough tossing, \\
        A little longer on the billows' foam; \\
        A few more journeyings in the desert darkness, \\
        And then the sunshine of thy Father's home.

    \item Not now; for I have wand'rers in the distance, \\
        And thou must call them in with patient love; \\
        Not now; for I have sheep upon the mountains, \\
        And thou must follow them where'er they rove.

    \item Not now; for I have loved ones sad and weary, \\
        Wilt thou not cheer them with a kindly smile? \\
        Sick ones, who need thee in their lonely sorrow--- \\
        Wilt thou not tend them yet a little while?

    \item Not now; for I have griefs for thee to witness, \\
        And heart-pains for thee in thy love to share; \\
        Not now; for I have lessons for thy learning, \\
        Before the glory thou art fit to wear.

    \item Not now; for I have wounds for thee to soften, \\
        And tears for thee to wipe from weeping eyes; \\
        Not now; for I have burdens for thy bearing, \\
        Before the dawn of heaven upon thee rise.

    \item Not now; for many a conflict yet awaits thee, \\
        And thou must conquer in the fearful fight; \\
        Not now; for many a soul must yet be gathered \\
        Before the fading of this earthly night.

    \item Go, with thy Saviour's hand to guide and strengthen, \\
        Until thy work is finished and complete; \\
        Then, with what joy shalt thou behold His glory, \\
        And lay thy trophies at the Master's feet!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 363}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D MORE TO FOLLOW}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HAVE you on the Lord believ\`ed? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Of His grace have you receiv\`ed? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Oh, the grace the Father shows, \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Freely He His grace bestows, \\
        Still there's more to follow.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{More and more, more and more,} \\
    \textit{Always more to follow;} \\
    \textit{Oh, His matchless, boundless love!} \\
    \textit{Still there's more to follow.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Have you felt the Saviour near you? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Does His bless\`ed presence cheer you? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Oh, the love the Saviour shows, \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Freely He His love bestows, \\
        Still there's more to follow.

    \item Have you known the Spirit's power? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Falling like the gentle shower? \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Oh, the peace the Spirit shows, \\
        Still there's more to follow; \\
        Freely He His peace bestows, \\
        Still there's more to follow.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 364}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D CHALDEA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HARK! the voice of Jesus calling, \\
        "Who will go and work to-day? \\
        Fields are white, and harvests waiting; \\
        Who will bear the sheaves away?" \\
        Loud and long the Master calleth, \\
        Rich reward He offers free; \\
        Who will answer, gladly saying, \\
        "Here am I; send me, send me"?

    \item If you cannot cross the ocean, \\
        And the heathen lands explore, \\
        You can find the heathen nearer, \\
        You can help them at your door. \\
        If you cannot give your thousands, \\
        You can give the widow's mite; \\
        And the least you do for Jesus \\
        Will be precious in His sight.

    \item If you cannot speak like angels, \\
        If you cannot preach like Paul, \\
        You can tell the love of Jesus, \\
        You can say He died for all. \\
        If you cannot rouse the wicked \\
        With the judgment's dread alarms, \\
        You can lead the little children \\
        To the Saviour's waiting arms.

    \item If you cannot be the watchman, \\
        Standing high on Zion's wall, \\
        Pointing out the path to heav\`en, \\
        Offering life and peace to all; \\
        With your prayers and with your bounties \\
        You can do what heav\`en demands; \\
        You can be like faithful Aaron, \\
        Holding up the prophet's hands.

    \item If, among the older people, \\
        You may not be apt to teach; \\
        "Feed My lambs," said Christ our Shepherd, \\
        "Place the food within their reach." \\
        And it may be that the children \\
        You have led with trembling hand \\
        Will be found among your jewels, \\
        When you reach the better land.

    \item Let none hear you idly saying, \\
        "There is nothing I can do," \\
        While the souls of men are dying, \\
        And the Master calls for you. \\
        Take the task He gives you gladly, \\
        Let His work your pleasure be; \\
        Answer quickly when He calleth, \\
        "Here am I; send me, send me."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 365}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. CRANBROOK}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate. The third line is repeated once by all parts, and the fourth line is repeated twice by all parts.]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item GRACE! 'tis a charming sound, \\
        Harmonious to the ear; \\
        Heav'n with the echo shall resound, \\
        And all the earth shall hear.

    \item 'Twas grace that wrote my name \\
        In life's eternal book; \\
        'Twas grace that gave me to the Lamb, \\
        Who all my sorrows took.

    \item Grace taught my wand'ring feet \\
        To tread the heav'nly road; \\
        And new supplies each hour I meet, \\
        While pressing on to God.

    \item Grace taught my soul to pray, \\
        And made mine eyes o'erflow; \\
        'Tis grace has kept me to this day, \\
        And will not let me go.

    \item Grace all the work shall crown \\
        Through everlasting days; \\
        It lays in heav'n the topmost stone, \\
        And well deserves the praise.

    \item Oh, let Thy grace inspire \\
        My soul with strength divine; \\
        May all my pow'rs to Thee aspire, \\
        And all my days be Thine.

    \item To Father, and to Son, \\
        And Spirit, One in Three, \\
        Be praise and adoration done \\
        Through all eternity.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 366}}
    \\
    \textit{6.6.4.6.6.6.4. MOSCOW}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THOU, whose almighty word \\
        Chaos and darkness heard, \\
        And took their flight, \\
        Hear us, we humbly pray, \\
        And where the gospel day \\
        Sheds not its glorious ray \\
        Let there be light.

    \item Thou, who didst come to bring \\
        On Thy redeeming wing \\
        Healing and sight, \\
        Be pleased, Lord, to unbind \\
        Sin's captives, sick in mind; \\
        And to the inly blind \\
        Let there be light.

    \item Spirit of truth and love, \\
        Life-giving, holy Dove, \\
        Speed forth Thy flight; \\
        Move on the waters' face \\
        By Thine almighty grace, \\
        And in earth's darkest place \\
        Let there be light.

    \item Bless\`ed and Holy Three, \\
        Thou glorious Trinity, \\
        Wisdom, Love, Might; \\
        Boundless as ocean's tide \\
        Rolling in fullest pride, \\
        O'er the world far and wide, \\
        Let there be light.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 367}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. MOUNT EPHRAIM}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SWEET is the work, my God, my King, \\
        To praise Thy name, give thanks and sing, \\
        To show Thy love by morning light, \\
        And talk of all Thy truth at night.

    \item Sweet is the day of sacred rest, \\
        No mortal cares shall seize my breast; \\
        Oh, may my heart in tune be found, \\
        Like David's harp of solemn sound.

    \item My heart shall triumph in my Lord, \\
        And bless His works, and bless His word; \\
        Thy works of grace, how bright they shine! \\
        How deep Thy counsels, how divine!

    \item Soon shall I see and hear and know \\
        All I desired or wished below; \\
        And every power find sweet employ \\
        In that eternal world of joy.

    \item Then shall I share a glorious part, \\
        When grace hath well refined my heart, \\
        And fresh supplies of joy are shed, \\
        Like holy oil, to cheer my head.

    \item Then shall I see, and hear, and know \\
        All I desired or wished below; \\
        And every power find sweet employ \\
        In that eternal world of joy.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 368}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. SHOWERS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THERE shall be showers of blessing: \\
        This is the promise of love; \\
        There shall be seasons refreshing, \\
        Sent from the Saviour above.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Showers of blessing,} \\
    \textit{Showers of blessing we need;} \\
    \textit{Mercy-drops round us are falling,} \\
    \textit{But for the showers we plead.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item There shall be showers of blessing, \\
        Precious reviving again; \\
        Over the hills and the valleys, \\
        Sound of abundance of rain.

    \item There shall be showers of blessing: \\
        Send them upon us, O Lord; \\
        Grant to us now a refreshing, \\
        Come, and honour Thy word.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 369}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. PENTECOST}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FIGHT the good fight with all thy might, \\
        Christ is thy strength, and Christ thy right; \\
        Lay hold on life, and it shall be \\
        Thy joy and crown eternally.

    \item Run the straight race through God's good grace, \\
        Lift up thine eyes, and seek His face; \\
        Life with its way before us lies, \\
        Christ is the path, and Christ the prize.

    \item Cast care aside, lean on thy Guide; \\
        His boundless mercy will provide; \\
        Trust, and thy trusting soul shall prove \\
        Christ is its life, and Christ its love.

    \item Faint not nor fear, His arms are near; \\
        He changeth not, and thou art dear; \\
        Only believe, and thou shalt see \\
        That Christ is all in all to thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 370}}
    \\
    \textit{6.4.6.4.6.7.6.4. WAKE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HARK! 'tis the watchman's cry, \\
        "Wake, brethren, wake!" \\
        Jesus our Lord is nigh; \\
        "Wake, brethren, wake!" \\
        Sleep is for sons of night; \\
        Ye are children of the light; \\
        Yours is the glory bright; \\
        "Wake, brethren, wake!"

    \item Call to each waking band, \\
        "Watch, brethren, watch!" \\
        Clear is our Lord's command, \\
        "Watch, brethren, watch!" \\
        Be ye as men that wait \\
        Always at the Master's gate, \\
        E'en though He tarry late; \\
        "Watch, brethren, watch!"

    \item Hear we the Saviour's voice, \\
        "Pray, brethren, pray!" \\
        Would ye His heart rejoice? \\
        "Pray, brethren, pray!" \\
        Sin calls for constant fear, \\
        Weakness needs the Helper near; \\
        He will your pleading hear; \\
        "Pray, brethren, pray!"

    \item Sound now the final chord, \\
        "Praise, brethren, praise!" \\
        Thrice holy is the Lord; \\
        "Praise, brethren, praise!" \\
        What more can hearts desire? \\
        Praise shall all our songs inspire, \\
        Lifting every spirit higher; \\
        "Praise, brethren, praise!"

    \item Now let the chorus rise, \\
        "Soon comes the King!" \\
        Look upward to the skies, \\
        "Soon comes the King!" \\
        Soon shall our sorrow cease, \\
        Soon shall begin heaven's peace; \\
        Lord, grant us rich increase; \\
        "Soon comes the King!"

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 371}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.5. D WORK}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WORK, for the night is coming, \\
        Work through the morning hours; \\
        Work while the dew is sparkling, \\
        Work 'mid springing flowers; \\
        Work when the day grows brighter, \\
        Work in the glowing sun; \\
        Work, for the night is coming, \\
        When man's work is done.

    \item Work, for the night is coming, \\
        Work through the sunny noon; \\
        Fill brightest hours with labour, \\
        Rest comes sure and soon; \\
        Give every flying minute \\
        Something to keep in store; \\
        Work, for the night is coming, \\
        When man works no more.

    \item Work, for the night is coming, \\
        Under the sunset skies; \\
        While their bright tints are glowing, \\
        Work, for daylight flies; \\
        Work till the last beam fadeth, \\
        Fadeth to shine no more; \\
        Work, while the night is darkening, \\
        When man's work is o'er.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 372}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D CALL THEM IN}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item "CALL them in!" the poor, the wretched, \\
        Sin-stained, wand'ring from the fold; \\
        Bidding them to share the banquet, \\
        Free for all, both young and old. \\
        "Call them in!" the Jew, the Gentile, \\
        Bid the stranger to the feast; \\
        "Call them in!" the rich, the noble, \\
        From the highest to the least.

    \item "Call them in!" the broken-hearted, \\
        Cowering 'neath the brand of shame; \\
        Speak the Master's free salvation, \\
        Life and pardon in His name. \\
        "Call them in!" the little children, \\
        Tarrying in the morning light; \\
        Call the young and call the aged, \\
        Ere they reach the shades of night.

    \item "Call them in!" the Master waiteth, \\
        Patient at the open door; \\
        See the banquet table spreadeth, \\
        Room for millions, room for more. \\
        "Call them in!" the angels listen \\
        To the joyful welcome song; \\
        Soon the harvest will be gathered, \\
        And the ransomed host shall throng.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 373}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D HEBER}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FROM Greenland's icy mountains, \\
        From India's coral strand, \\
        Where Afric's sunny fountains \\
        Roll down their golden sand, \\
        From many an ancient river, \\
        From many a palmy plain, \\
        They call us to deliver \\
        Their land from error's chain.

    \item What though the spicy breezes \\
        Blow soft o'er Ceylon's isle; \\
        Though every prospect pleases, \\
        And only man is vile; \\
        In vain with lavish kindness \\
        The gifts of God are strown; \\
        The heathen in his blindness \\
        Bows down to wood and stone.

    \item Can we, whose souls are lighted \\
        With wisdom from on high, \\
        Can we to men benighted \\
        The lamp of life deny? \\
        Salvation! O salvation! \\
        The joyful sound proclaim, \\
        Till each remotest nation \\
        Has learnt Messiah's name.

    \item Waft, waft, ye winds, His story, \\
        And you, ye waters, roll, \\
        Till, like a sea of glory, \\
        It spreads from pole to pole; \\
        Till o'er our ransomed nature \\
        The Lamb for sinners slain, \\
        Redeemer, King, Creator, \\
        In bliss returns to reign.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 374}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. GIDEON}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JESUS shall reign where'er the sun \\
        Does his successive journeys run; \\
        His kingdom stretch from shore to shore, \\
        Till moons shall wax and wane no more.

    \item For Him shall endless prayer be made, \\
        And praises throng to crown His head; \\
        His name like sweet perfume shall rise \\
        With every morning sacrifice.

    \item People and realms of every tongue \\
        Dwell on His love with sweetest song; \\
        And infant voices shall proclaim \\
        Their early blessings on His name.

    \item Blessings abound where'er He reigns; \\
        The prisoner leaps to lose his chains, \\
        The weary find eternal rest, \\
        And all the sons of want are blest.

    \item Where He displays His healing power, \\
        Death and the curse are known no more; \\
        In Him the tribes of Adam boast \\
        More blessings than their father lost.

    \item Let every creature rise and bring \\
        Peculiar honours to our King; \\
        Angels descend with songs again, \\
        And earth repeat the long Amen.

    \item Praise God, from whom all blessings flow; \\
        Praise Him, all creatures here below; \\
        Praise Him above, ye heav'nly host; \\
        Praise Father, Son, and Holy Ghost.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 375}}
    \\
    \textit{D.C.M. ELLACOMBE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I'M not ashamed to own my Lord, \\
        Or to defend His cause, \\
        Maintain the glory of His cross, \\
        And honour all His laws. \\
        Jesus, my God! I know His name; \\
        His name is all my trust; \\
        Nor will He put my soul to shame, \\
        Nor let my hope be lost.

    \item Firm as His throne His promise stands, \\
        And He can well secure \\
        What I've committed to His hands \\
        Till the decisive hour. \\
        Then will He own my worthless name \\
        Before His Father's face, \\
        And in the New Jerusalem \\
        Appoint my soul a place.

    \item Beyond the glittering, starry skies, \\
        Far as th' eternal hills, \\
        There, in the boundless realms of light, \\
        Our great Redeemer dwells. \\
        Legions of angels strong and bright \\
        Around Him constant stand; \\
        They wait His pleasure, do His will, \\
        And fly at His command.

    \item Hail, Prince of life! for ever hail! \\
        Redeemer, Brother, Friend! \\
        Though earth and time and life shall fail, \\
        Thy praise shall never end. \\
        All glory to the Father be, \\
        All glory to the Son, \\
        All glory to the Holy Ghost, \\
        While endless ages run.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 376}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M. COVENANTERS}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item AM I a soldier of the cross, \\
        A follower of the Lamb, \\
        And shall I fear to own His cause, \\
        Or blush to speak His name?

    \item Must I be carried to the skies \\
        On flowery beds of ease, \\
        While others fight to win the prize, \\
        And sail through stormy seas?

    \item Are there no foes for me to face? \\
        Must I not stem the flood? \\
        Is this vile world a friend to grace, \\
        To help me on to God?

    \item Sure I must fight if I would win; \\
        Increase my courage, Lord! \\
        I'll bear the toil, endure the pain, \\
        Supported by Thy word.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 377}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. GALILEE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JESUS calls us! o'er the tumult \\
        Of our life's wild, restless sea, \\
        Day by day His sweet voice soundeth, \\
        Saying, "Christian, follow Me."

    \item As of old Saint Andrew heard it \\
        By the Galilean lake, \\
        Turned from home and toil and kindred, \\
        Leaving all for His dear sake.

    \item Jesus calls us from the worship \\
        Of the vain world's golden store, \\
        From each idol that would keep us, \\
        Saying, "Christian, love Me more."

    \item In our joys and in our sorrows, \\
        Days of toil and hours of ease, \\
        Still He calls, in cares and pleasures, \\
        "Christian, love Me more than these."

    \item Jesus calls us! By Thy mercies, \\
        Saviour, may we hear Thy call, \\
        Give our hearts to Thine obedience, \\
        Serve and love Thee best of all.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 378}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.8.8. PATER OMNIUM}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LEAD, kindly Light, amid the encircling gloom, \\
        Lead Thou me on; \\
        The night is dark, and I am far from home, \\
        Lead Thou me on; \\
        Keep Thou my feet; I do not ask to see \\
        The distant scene---one step enough for me.

    \item I was not ever thus, nor prayed that Thou \\
        Shouldst lead me on; \\
        I loved to choose and see my path; but now \\
        Lead Thou me on. \\
        I loved the garish day, and, spite of fears, \\
        Pride ruled my will: remember not past years.

    \item So long Thy power hath blest me, sure it still \\
        Will lead me on \\
        O'er moor and fen, o'er crag and torrent, till \\
        The night is gone, \\
        And with the morn those angel faces smile \\
        Which I have loved long since, and lost awhile.

    \item Meantime along the narrow rugged path \\
        Thyself hast trod, \\
        Lead, Saviour, lead the weary wanderer on \\
        Home to his God; \\
        Rememb\`ering, in each dark and cloudy hour, \\
        Thy love, Thy faithfulness, Thy saving power.

    \item Then, when the night is o'er, and all our tears \\
        Are wiped away, \\
        We'll praise Thee, Lord, through all eternity, \\
        In perfect day; \\
        Where sorrow, sighing, pain and death shall cease, \\
        And all be love and joy and heavenly peace.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 379}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. ACCRINGTON}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item AWAKE, our souls; away, our fears; \\
        Let every trembling thought be gone; \\
        Awake, and run the heavenly race, \\
        And put a cheerful courage on.

    \item True, 'tis a strait and thorny road, \\
        And mortal spirits tire and faint; \\
        But they forget the mighty God, \\
        Who feeds the strength of every saint.

    \item The mighty God, whose matchless power \\
        Is ever new and ever young, \\
        And firm endures, while endless years \\
        Their everlasting circles run.

    \item From Thee, the overflowing spring, \\
        Our souls shall drink a fresh supply, \\
        While such as trust their native strength \\
        Shall melt away, and drop, and die.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 380}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. BLOCKLEY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O MASTER, let me walk with Thee \\
        In lowly paths of service free; \\
        Tell me Thy secret; help me bear \\
        The strain of toil, the fret of care.

    \item Help me the slow of heart to move \\
        By some clear, winning word of love; \\
        They wayward feet to guide and stay, \\
        And lead them in the narrow way.

    \item Teach me Thy patience; still with Thee \\
        In closer, dearer company, \\
        In work that keeps faith sweet and strong, \\
        In trust that triumphs over wrong.

    \item In hope that sends a shining ray \\
        Far down the future's broad'ning way; \\
        In peace that only Thou canst give, \\
        With Thee, O Master, let me live.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 381}}
    \\
    \textit{6.5.6.5. D ASPIRATION}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item MORE holiness give me, more strivings within, \\
        More patience in suffering, more sorrow for sin; \\
        More faith in my Saviour, more sense of His care, \\
        More joy in His service, more purpose in prayer.

    \item More gratitude give me, more trust in the Lord, \\
        More zeal for His glory, more hope in His word; \\
        More tears for His sorrows, more pain at His grief, \\
        More meekness in tri\`al, more praise for relief.

    \item More purity give me, more strength to o'ercome, \\
        More freedom from earth-stains, more longings for home; \\
        More fit for the kingdom, more used would I be, \\
        More bless\`ed and holy, more, Saviour, like Thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 382}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. D CALON L\^{A}N}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HEAVENLY Father, may Thy blessing \\
        Rest upon Thy children now, \\
        When in praise Thy name we hallow, \\
        When in prayer to Thee we bow. \\
        In the wondrous story reading \\
        Of the Lord of truth and grace, \\
        May we see Thy love reflected \\
        In the light of His dear face.

    \item May we learn from this great story \\
        All the arts of friendliness, \\
        Truthful speech and honest action, \\
        Courage, patience, steadfastness; \\
        How to master self and temper, \\
        How to make our conduct fair; \\
        When to speak and when be silent, \\
        When to do and when forbear.

    \item May His Spirit, wise and holy, \\
        With His gifts our spirits bless, \\
        Make us loving, joyous, peaceful, \\
        Rich in goodness, gentleness, \\
        Strong in self-control, and faithful, \\
        Kind in thought and deed; for He \\
        Sayeth, "What ye do for others \\
        Ye are doing unto Me."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 383}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. RHODES}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SOW in the morn thy seed, \\
        At eve hold not thy hand; \\
        To doubt and fear give thou no heed, \\
        Broadcast it o'er the land.

    \item Thou know'st not which may thrive, \\
        The late or early sown; \\
        Grace keeps the chosen germ alive, \\
        When and wherever strewn.

    \item And duly shall appear \\
        In verdure, beauty, strength, \\
        The tender blade, the stalk, the ear, \\
        And the full corn at length.

    \item Thou canst not toil in vain; \\
        Cold, heat, and moist, and dry \\
        Shall foster and mature the grain \\
        For garners in the sky.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 384}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.6. ST. MARGARET}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O LOVE, that wilt not let me go, \\
        I rest my weary soul in Thee; \\
        I give Thee back the life I owe, \\
        That in Thine ocean depths its flow \\
        May richer, fuller be.

    \item O Light, that foll'west all my way, \\
        I yield my flickering torch to Thee; \\
        My heart restores its borrowed ray, \\
        That in Thy sunshine's blaze its day \\
        May brighter, fairer be.

    \item O Joy, that seekest me through pain, \\
        I cannot close my heart to Thee; \\
        I trace the rainbow through the rain, \\
        And feel the promise is not vain \\
        That morn shall tearless be.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 385}}
    \\
    \textit{6.5.6.5. D RACHIE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WHO is on the Lord's side? \\
        Who will serve the King? \\
        Who will be His helpers, \\
        Other lives to bring? \\
        Who will leave the world's side? \\
        Who will face the foe? \\
        Who is on the Lord's side? \\
        Who for Him will go? \\
        By Thy great redemption, \\
        By Thy grace divine, \\
        We are on the Lord's side; \\
        Saviour, we are Thine!

    \item Not for weight of glory, \\
        Not for crown or palm, \\
        Enter we the army, \\
        Raise the warrior's psalm; \\
        But for love that claimeth \\
        Lives for whom He died--- \\
        He Christ Jesus nameth \\
        Must be on His side!

    \item Thou, O Lord, hast bought us, \\
        Not with gold or gem, \\
        But with Thine own life blood, \\
        For Thy diadem. \\
        With Thy blessing filling \\
        All who come to Thee, \\
        Thou hast made us willing, \\
        Thou hast made us free.

    \item Fierce may be the conflict, \\
        Strong may be the foe, \\
        But the King's own army \\
        None can overthrow; \\
        Round His standard ranging, \\
        Victory is secure, \\
        For His truth unchanging \\
        Makes the triumph sure.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 386}}
    \\
    \textit{Irregular RESOLUTION}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HAVE ye counted the cost, \\
        Ye warriors of the cross? \\
        Are ye fixed in your heart for the Master's sake \\
        To suffer all earthly loss? \\
        Can ye bear the scoff of the worldly wise, \\
        As ye pass by pleasure's bower, \\
        To watch with the Lord on the mountain top \\
        Through the dreary midnight hour?

    \item Ye may drink of His cup, \\
        And in His baptism share, \\
        Ye shall not fail, if ye tread in His steps, \\
        His blood-stained cross to bear. \\
        But count ye the cost, oh count ye the cost, \\
        That ye be not unprepared; \\
        And know ye the strength that alone can stand \\
        In the conflict ye have dared.

    \item In the power of His might, \\
        Who was made through weakness strong, \\
        Ye shall overcome in the fearful fight \\
        And sing His victory song. \\
        By the blood of the Lamb, by the blood of the Lamb, \\
        By the faithful witness word, \\
        Not loving your lives unto death for Him, \\
        Ye shall triumph with your Lord.

    \item Oh, the banner of love, \\
        It will cost you a pang to hold, \\
        But 'twill float in triumph the field above, \\
        Though your hearts' blood stain its fold. \\
        Ye may count the cost, ye may count the cost \\
        Of all of Egypt's gold, \\
        But the riches of Christ ye cannot count, \\
        His love cannot be told.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 387}}
    \\
    \textit{7.5.7.6. DANIEL}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item STANDING by a purpose true, \\
        Heeding God's command, \\
        Honour them, the faithful few! \\
        All hail to Daniel's band!

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Dare to be a Daniel!} \\
    \textit{Dare to stand alone!} \\
    \textit{Dare to have a purpose firm!} \\
    \textit{Dare to make it known!}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Many mighty men are lost, \\
        Daring not to stand, \\
        Who for God had been a host, \\
        By joining Daniel's band.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 388}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. ST. ETHELWALD}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item SOLDIERS of Christ, arise \\
        And put your armour on, \\
        Strong in the strength which God supplies \\
        Through His eternal Son;

    \item Strong in the Lord of hosts, \\
        And in His mighty power, \\
        Who in the strength of Jesus trusts \\
        Is more than conqu\`eror.

    \item Stand then in His great might, \\
        With all His strength endued; \\
        And take, to arm you for the fight, \\
        The panoply of God.

    \item Leave no unguarded place, \\
        No weakness of the soul; \\
        Take every virtue, every grace, \\
        And fortify the whole;

    \item That having all things done, \\
        And all your conflicts passed, \\
        The victory won through Christ alone, \\
        To dwell with Him at last.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 389}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D CR\"{U}GER}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item BLEST Saviour, we would own Thee \\
        Amid the world's proud scorn, \\
        The world that mocked, and crowned Thee \\
        With diadem of thorn. \\
        The world that now rejects Thee, \\
        Makes nothing of Thy love, \\
        Counts not the grace and pity \\
        That brought Thee from above.

    \item O Lord, O Master, help us \\
        To walk apart with Thee, \\
        Outside the camp, where only \\
        Thy beauty we may see; \\
        Far from the world's loud turmoil, \\
        Far from its busy din, \\
        Far from its praise and honour, \\
        Its unbelief and sin.

    \item Oh, keep each heart at leisure \\
        From all the world beside, \\
        In close communion ever \\
        Thus with Thee to abide; \\
        So all Thy whispered breathings \\
        Of love and truth to hear, \\
        And hail Thee with rejoicing \\
        When Thou shalt soon appear.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 390}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D MORNING LIGHT}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item STAND bravely for your Master, \\
        Ye soldiers of the cross; \\
        Lift high His royal banner, \\
        It must not suffer loss. \\
        From victory unto victory \\
        His army will He lead, \\
        Till every foe is vanquished, \\
        And Christ is Lord indeed.

    \item Stand bravely for your Master, \\
        The trumpet call obey, \\
        Forth to the mighty conflict \\
        In this most glorious day. \\
        Ye that are men now serve Him \\
        Against unnumbered foes; \\
        Let courage rise with danger, \\
        And strength to strength oppose.

    \item Stand bravely for your Master, \\
        Stand in His strength alone; \\
        The arm of flesh will fail you, \\
        Ye dare not trust your own. \\
        Put on the gospel armour, \\
        And, watching unto prayer, \\
        Where duty calls to danger \\
        Be never wanting there.

    \item Stand bravely for your Master, \\
        The strife will not be long; \\
        This day the noise of battle, \\
        The next the victor's song. \\
        To him that overcometh \\
        A crown of life shall be; \\
        He with the King of glory \\
        Shall reign eternally.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 391}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. PAULINA}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WE'RE marching to Canaan with banner and song, \\
        We're soldiers enlisted to fight 'gainst the wrong; \\
        But lest in the conflict our strength should divide, \\
        We ask, "Who among us is on the Lord's side?"

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Oh, who is there among us, the true and the tried,} \\
    \textit{Who'll stand by his colours; who's on the Lord's side?}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item The sword may be burnished, the armour be bright \\
        (E'en Satan appears as an angel of light), \\
        Yet darkly the bosom may treach\`ery hide, \\
        While lips are professing, "I'm on the Lord's side."

    \item Oh, is one among us who cleaves not to God, \\
        Whose heart follows not in the path to be trod? \\
        Let him learn, ere the darkness his footsteps betide, \\
        That light, life and peace are upon the Lord's side.

    \item Oh, heed not the sorrow, the pain or the wrong, \\
        For soon shall our sighing be changed into song; \\
        So, bearing the cross of our cov\`enant Guide, \\
        We'll shout, as we triumph, "I'm on the Lord's side."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 392}}
    \\
    \textit{11.11.11.11. WATCHWORD}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast. The fifth line is repeated twice by the Treble and once by the Alto.]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item O CHRISTIAN, awake! for the strife is at hand; \\
        With helmet and shield, and a sword in thy hand, \\
        To meet the bold tempter, go, fearlessly go, \\
        And stand, like the brave, with thy face to the foe. \\
        Stand like the brave, \\
        With thy face to the foe!

    \item The cause of thy Master with vigour defend, \\
        Be watchful, be zealous, and fight to the end; \\
        Wherever He leads thee, go, valiantly go, \\
        And stand, like the brave, with thy face to the foe. \\
        Stand like the brave, \\
        With thy face to the foe!

    \item Press on, never doubting; thy Captain is near, \\
        With grace to supply and with comfort to cheer. \\
        His love, like a stream in the desert, will flow, \\
        Then stand, like the brave, with thy face to the foe. \\
        Stand like the brave, \\
        With thy face to the foe!

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 393}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.3. VIGILATE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item CHRISTIAN, seek not yet repose, \\
        Cast thy dreams of ease away; \\
        Thou art in the midst of foes--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

    \item Principalities and powers, \\
        Mustering their unseen array, \\
        Wait for thine unguarded hours--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

    \item Gird thy heavenly armour on, \\
        Wear it ever night and day; \\
        Ambushed lies the evil one--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

    \item Hear the victors who o'ercame; \\
        Still they mark each warrior's way; \\
        All with one clear voice exclaim--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

    \item Hear, above all, hear thy Lord, \\
        Him thou lovest to obey; \\
        Hide within thy heart His word--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

    \item Watch, as if on that alone \\
        Hung the issue of the day; \\
        Pray that help may be sent down--- \\
        "Watch and pray."

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 394}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. ST. ALPHEGE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item BRIEF life is here our portion, \\
        Brief sorrow, short-lived care; \\
        The life that knows no ending, \\
        The tearless life is there.

    \item Oh, happy retribution--- \\
        Short toil, eternal rest; \\
        For cleansed and ransomed sinners \\
        A mansion with the blest.

    \item For now we fight the battle, \\
        But then shall wear the crown \\
        Of full and everlasting \\
        And passionless renown.

    \item And now we watch and struggle, \\
        And now we live in hope; \\
        And Zion in her anguish \\
        With Babylon must cope.

    \item But He whom now we trust in, \\
        Shall then be seen and known; \\
        And they that know and see Him \\
        Shall have Him for their own.

    \item The morning shall awaken, \\
        The shadows pass away, \\
        And each true-hearted servant \\
        Shall shine as doth the day.

    \item There God our bless\`ed portion, \\
        In fulness of His grace, \\
        We shall behold for ever \\
        And worship face to face.

    \item Then quickly come, Lord Jesus, \\
        And take Thy people home; \\
        We long for Thy returning, \\
        Lord Jesus, quickly come! Amen.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\thispagestyle{empty}
\vspace*{3.5cm}
\begin{center}
    {\LARGE \textbf{SECTION 15}}\\[0.8em]
    {\Large \textsc{For Young Disciples}}
\end{center}
\vspace{3.5cm}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 395}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. ST. OSWALD}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JESUS Christ, my Lord and Saviour, \\
        Once became a child like me; \\
        Oh, that in my whole behaviour \\
        He my pattern still might be!

    \item All my nature is unholy, \\
        Pride and passion dwell within; \\
        But the Lord was meek and lowly, \\
        And was never known to sin.

    \item While I'm often vainly trying \\
        Some new pleasure to possess, \\
        He was always self-denying, \\
        Patient in His worst distress.

    \item Let me never be forgetful \\
        Of His precepts any more, \\
        Idle, passionate, nor fretful, \\
        As I've often been before.

    \item Lord, though now Thou art in glory, \\
        I have Thine example still; \\
        I can read Thy sacred story, \\
        And obey Thy holy will.

    \item Help me by that rule to measure \\
        Every word and every thought, \\
        Thinking it my greatest pleasure, \\
        There to learn what Thou hast taught.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 396}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. CALM}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item GREAT God, and dost Thou condescend \\
        To be my Father and my Friend? \\
        I, a poor child, and Thou so high, \\
        The Lord of earth and sea and sky!

    \item Yes, wondrous God, for I received \\
        Thy Son, and on His name believed. \\
        Now I'm a true-born child of Thine, \\
        And Thou, His Father, too art mine.

    \item Thou art my Father; Thou canst bear \\
        To hear my poor imperfect prayer, \\
        And Thou wilt listen to the praise \\
        That I, a little one, can raise.

    \item Thou art my Father; let me be \\
        A meek, obedient child to Thee, \\
        And try, in word and deed and thought, \\
        To serve and please Thee as I ought.

    \item Thou art my Father; I'll depend \\
        Upon the care of such a Friend, \\
        And only wish to do and be \\
        Whatever seemeth good to Thee.

    \item Then Thou, my Father, wilt at last, \\
        When all my days on earth are past, \\
        Receive me to my home above, \\
        To rest for ever in Thy love.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 397}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7.7.7. IRBY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item ONCE in royal David's city \\
        Stood a lowly cattle shed, \\
        Where a mother laid her Baby \\
        In a manger for His bed; \\
        Mary was that mother mild, \\
        Christ the Lord her little Child.

    \item He came down to earth from heav\`en, \\
        Who is God and Lord of all; \\
        And His shelter was a stable, \\
        And His cradle was a stall; \\
        With the poor and mean and lowly, \\
        Lived on earth our Saviour holy.

    \item And through all His wondrous childhood, \\
        He would honour and obey, \\
        Love and watch the lowly mother, \\
        In whose gentle arms He lay; \\
        Christian children all must be \\
        Mild, obedient, good like He.

    \item For He is our childhood's pattern, \\
        As we grow, He daily grew; \\
        He was little, weak, and helpless, \\
        Tears and smiles like us He knew; \\
        And He feeleth for our sadness, \\
        And He shareth in our gladness.

    \item And our eyes at last shall see Him, \\
        Through His own redeeming love; \\
        For that Child, so meek and gentle, \\
        Is our Lord in heaven above; \\
        And He leads His people on \\
        To the place where He is gone.

    \item Not in that poor lowly stable, \\
        With the oxen standing by, \\
        We shall see Him; but in heav\`en, \\
        Set at God's right hand on high; \\
        When like stars His people crowned, \\
        All in white shall stand around.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 398}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. D ANGELS' STORY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I LOVE to hear the story \\
        Which angel voices tell, \\
        How once the King of glory \\
        Came down on earth to dwell. \\
        I am both weak and sinful, \\
        But this I surely know, \\
        The Lord came down to save me, \\
        Because He loves me so.

    \item I'm glad the bless\`ed Saviour \\
        Was once a child like me, \\
        To show how pure and holy \\
        His little one should be. \\
        And I will try to follow \\
        His footsteps here below; \\
        For He will ne'er forget me, \\
        Because He loves me so.

    \item To sing His love and mercy, \\
        My sweetest songs I'll raise; \\
        And though I cannot see Him, \\
        I know He hears my praise; \\
        For He has kindly promised \\
        That I shall surely go \\
        To sing with Him in heav\`en, \\
        Because He loves me so.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 399}}
    \\
    \textit{11.8.11.8. HE HIDETH MY SOUL}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item A WONDERFUL Saviour is Jesus my Lord, \\
        A wonderful Saviour to me; \\
        He hideth my soul in the cleft of the rock, \\
        Where rivers of pleasure I see.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{He hideth my soul in the cleft of the rock,} \\
    \textit{That shadows a dry, thirsty land;} \\
    \textit{He hideth my life in the depths of His love,} \\
    \textit{And covers me there with His hand.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item A wonderful Saviour is Jesus my Lord, \\
        He taketh my burden away; \\
        He holdeth me up and I shall not be moved; \\
        He giveth me strength as my day.

    \item With numberless blessings each moment He crowns, \\
        And, filled with His goodness divine, \\
        I sing in my rapture, Oh glory to God \\
        For such a Redeemer as mine!

    \item When, clothed in His brightness, transported I rise \\
        To meet Him in clouds of the sky, \\
        His perfect salvation, His wonderful love, \\
        I'll shout with the millions on high.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 400}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.8.8.8. MOZART}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item WE saw Thee not when Thou didst tread, \\
        O Saviour, this our sinful earth; \\
        Nor heard Thy voice restore the dead, \\
        And wake them to a second birth; \\
        But we believe that Thou didst come, \\
