        And quit for us Thy glorious home.

    \item We were not with the faithful few \\
        Who stood Thy bitter cross around, \\
        Nor heard Thy prayer for those who slew, \\
        Nor felt the earthquake rock the ground. \\
        We saw no spear-wound pierce Thy side; \\
        Yet we believe that Thou hast died.

    \item No angel's message met our ear \\
        On that first glorious Lordly day, \\
        "The Lord is risen, He is not here, \\
        Come see the place where Jesus lay;" \\
        But we believe that Thou didst quell \\
        The banded powers of death and hell.

    \item We saw not Thee return on high, \\
        And now, our longing sight to bless, \\
        No rays of glory from the sky \\
        Shine down upon our wilderness; \\
        Yet we believe that Thou art there, \\
        And seek Thee, Lord, in praise and prayer.

    \item We heard Thee not when Thou didst say, \\
        "I leave you now, but soon will come \\
        And take you from this world away \\
        To dwell with Me in My bright home;" \\
        But we believe Thou'lt soon return; \\
        To see Thee, Lord, our spirits yearn.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 401}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.6. SAFFRON WALDEN}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderately fast]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JUST as I am, Thine own to be, \\
        Friend of the young, who lovest me, \\
        To consecrate myself to Thee, \\
        Lord Jesus Christ, I come.

    \item In the glad morning of my day, \\
        My life to give, my vows to pay, \\
        With no reserve and no delay, \\
        With all my heart, I come.

    \item I would live ever in the light, \\
        I would work ever for the right, \\
        I would serve Thee with all my might, \\
        Therefore to Thee I come.

    \item Just as I am, young, strong and free, \\
        To be the best that I can be \\
        For truth and righteousness and Thee, \\
        Lord of my life, I come.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 402}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.8.7. CARRADALE}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item I AM not skilled to understand \\
        What God hath willed, what God hath planned; \\
        I only know at His right hand \\
        Stands One who is my Saviour.

    \item I take Him at His word indeed--- \\
        Christ died to save me, this I read; \\
        For in my heart I find a need \\
        Of Him to be my Saviour.

    \item That He should leave His place on high \\
        And come for sinful man to die--- \\
        You count it strange? So once did I, \\
        Before I knew my Saviour.

    \item And oh, that He fulfilled may see \\
        The travail of His soul, in me; \\
        And with His work contented be, \\
        As I with my dear Saviour.

    \item Yea, living, dying, let me bring \\
        My strength, my solace from this spring--- \\
        That He who lives to be my King \\
        Once died to be my Saviour.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 403}}
    \\
    \textit{7.7.7.7.7.7. JESUS, SAVIOUR, PILOT ME}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Slow]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item LIFE and light and joy are found \\
        In the presence of the Lord; \\
        Life, with richest blessings crowned, \\
        Light, from many fountains poured; \\
        Life and light and holy joy \\
        None can darken or destroy.

    \item Bring to Him life's brightest hours, \\
        He will make them still more bright; \\
        Give to Him your noblest powers, \\
        He will hallow all your might; \\
        Come to Him with eager quest, \\
        You shall hear His high behest.

    \item All your questions large and deep, \\
        All the open thoughts of youth, \\
        Bring to Him, and you shall reap \\
        All the harvest of His truth; \\
        You shall find in that great store \\
        Largest love and wisest lore.

    \item Then, when comes life's wider sphere \\
        And its busier enterprise, \\
        You shall find Him ever near, \\
        Looking with approving eyes \\
        On all honest work and true \\
        That His servant's hands can do.

    \item And if care should dim your eye, \\
        And life's shadows come apace, \\
        You shall find Him ever nigh \\
        In His all-abounding grace, \\
        Changing sorrow's darkest night \\
        Into morning clear and bright.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 404}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7. CHANNELS ONLY}
    \\
    \textit{\small [Moderate]}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item HOW I praise Thee, precious Saviour, \\
        That Thy love laid hold of me! \\
        Thou hast saved and cleansed and filled me, \\
        That I might Thy channel be.

    \item[] \vspace{0.4em}
    \textbf{\textit{[Chorus]}} \\
    \textit{Channels only, bless\`ed Master,} \\
    \textit{But with all Thy wondrous power} \\
    \textit{Flowing through us, Thou canst use us} \\
    \textit{Every day and every hour.}
    \vspace{0.4em}

    \item Just a channel, full of blessing \\
        To the thirsty hearts around; \\
        To tell out Thy full salvation, \\
        All Thy loving message sound.

    \item Emptied that Thou shouldest fill me, \\
        A clean vessel in Thine hand; \\
        With no power but as Thou givest \\
        Graciously with each command.

    \item Witnessing Thy power to save me, \\
        Setting free from self and sin; \\
        Thou hast bought me to possess me; \\
        In Thy fulness, Lord, come in.

    \item Jesus, fill now with Thy Spirit \\
        Hearts that full surrender know; \\
        That the streams of living water \\
        From our inner man may flow.

\end{enumerate}

\thispagestyle{empty}
\vspace*{3.5cm}
\begin{center}
    {\LARGE \textbf{SECTION 16}}\\[0.8em]
    {\Large \textsc{The Place of the Name}}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 405}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M.D. HEREFORD}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item How truly lovely are Thy tents, \\
        O Lord of hosts, to me! \\
        My longing soul faints for Thy courts, \\
        My heart cries out to Thee. \\
        The sparrow and the swallow there \\
        Find for their young a nest; \\
        Thine altars, O my King and God, \\
        Provide for them a rest.

    \item Who in Thy house abide are blest, \\
        They will be praising still; \\
        And blest is he who, trusting, loves \\
        The ways to Zion's hill. \\
        Joy-springs and blessings mark their path \\
        Along this vale of tears; \\
        With strength renewed before their God \\
        In Zion each appears.

    \item Thine ear to my petitions, Lord, \\
        Thou God of Jacob, yield. \\
        Look now on Thine Anointed's face; \\
        Behold, O God, our shield. \\
        A day that's spent within Thy courts \\
        A thousand ne'er excel; \\
        I'd rather keep a door therein, \\
        Than with the wicked dwell.

    \item Himself to be a sun and shield, \\
        The Lord, our God, doth show. \\
        Unstinted grace and glory, too, \\
        He surely will bestow. \\
        No good from them will He withhold, \\
        Who walk now perfectly; \\
        Yea, bless\`ed is the man who trusts, \\
        O Lord of hosts, in Thee.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 406}}
    \\
    \textit{8.7.8.7.D BETHANY}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item God is faithful, who forgave us \\
        All the ills we e'er had done, \\
        And in pity deigned to save us \\
        For the sake of His dear Son. \\
        God will through eternal ages \\
        Not one sin remember more; \\
        Blotted from th'accusing pages, \\
        Nought the writing can restore.

    \item God is faithful, He has raised us \\
        From the dust to glory high; \\
        In His Christ secure has placed us, \\
        Never more to fall and die. \\
        Bonds of love thus forged in heav\`en, \\
        Nothing, no one, can disrupt; \\
        Wealth of heavenly blessings giv\`en \\
        Rests where nothing can corrupt.

    \item God is faithful, He whose word is \\
        Building up in unity \\
        Saints who own His Christ their Lord is \\
        In the one Community. \\
        These the Father ne'er forgetteth, \\
        Who the Son thus honour here; \\
        In His book their names He setteth, \\
        Gems for Him to honour there.

    \item God is faithful. Deem no burden \\
        He imposes hard to bear. \\
        Faith beholds the bless\`ed guerdon, \\
        Rest eternal waiting there. \\
        Ye, who for the faith contending, \\
        God-equipped are warring now, \\
        See the faithful God extending \\
        Victor-wreaths to grace your brow.

    \item God is faithful, He will ever \\
        By His promises abide. \\
        Human oaths may break, but never \\
        Fails His word whate'er betide. \\
        All your fears His word relieving, \\
        On, ye saints, your Lord to meet; \\
        Gladly then, your crowns receiving, \\
        Haste to cast them at His feet.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 407}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M.D. ELIM}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item Lo! men deem fitting to adorn \\
        The kingly Sufferer's brow, \\
        Not with gemmed gold, but wreathen thorn, \\
        Then in derision bow. \\
        With bitter taunt they haste the King, \\
        Not to a glorious throne, \\
        But to the cross the Lord they bring, \\
        And there His claims disown.

    \item Now myriads own, above the skies, \\
        His right divine to reign, \\
        But still the guilty world denies \\
        His title with disdain. \\
        Yet there's a place God set apart \\
        Where He may reign on earth; \\
        'Tis in the willing-opened heart \\
        Of one of heavenly birth.

    \item That heart, which He so dearly bought, \\
        And cleansed by precious blood, \\
        The fount of every evil thought, \\
        Once in rebellion stood. \\
        Oh, wonder! that the Lord should choose \\
        This heart to be His throne. \\
        Can I such honour e'er refuse, \\
        Or spurn the grace He's shown?

    \item Take, Lord, Thy rightful place within, \\
        And there supremely reign, \\
        That never more oppressing sin \\
        Dominion may obtain. \\
        Rule now 'midst subject hearts, O Lord, \\
        That so this sterile earth \\
        May one green spot for Thee afford \\
        In all its scene of dearth.

    \item Soon shall Thy feet in triumph stand \\
        Again on Olive's brow, \\
        When men and kings of every land \\
        At Thy great name shall bow. \\
        Meanwhile, O Lord, Thy sceptre wield \\
        Among Thy loyal few, \\
        Till, as in heaven, all earth shall yield \\
        To Thee Thy royal due.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 408}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6. KOCHER}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THE high ways we have traversed, \\
        And come to Zion's hill, \\
        Where God, our God, is with us, \\
        His purpose to fulfil.

    \item Here are God's house and altar, \\
        The place of His great name, \\
        Where praise to Him ascendeth, \\
        Where we His grace acclaim.

    \item 'Tis here a holy priesthood \\
        Can look upon His face, \\
        And hence a royal priesthood \\
        Proclaims His matchless grace.

    \item This house for God to dwell in, \\
        Of ransomed saints is built, \\
        Who by the great atonement \\
        Are purged and free from guilt.

    \item We come to Christ, the Chosen \\
        (The living stone is He), \\
        Through Him to God the Father, \\
        To make sweet melody.

    \item And forth we'd go unfearing \\
        The gospel to proclaim; \\
        The Lord Himself is with us \\
        As we exalt His name.

    \item Earth's days will soon be over, \\
        The day of Christ will come; \\
        Come quickly, Lord, to take us \\
        To Thy bright heavenly home.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 409}}
    \\
    \textit{6.6.6.6.8.8. GOPSAL}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item JEHOVAH Elohim, \\
        Thou Father and Thou Son \\
        And Holy Spirit too \\
        So perfectly are one. \\
        Thy purposes and works agree \\
        In an eternal unity.

    \item Thou Author of our lives, \\
        The counsels of Thy mind \\
        Decreed our union, \\
        And Thou in one didst bind \\
        Our lives in Christ inseparable, \\
        A Body indivisible.

    \item 'Tis precious too, O God, \\
        Thine eye should look upon \\
        Thy scattered children here \\
        United into one. \\
        Lord, may Thy word for ever be \\
        Our guide to perfect unity.

    \item Grant that more perfectly \\
        Thy will may be expressed, \\
        That gathered saints may show \\
        Thy way of truth is best. \\
        So mid this discord men may see \\
        Divinely ordered unity.

    \item Light and discernment give \\
        Those who are unaware \\
        Dark error binds their feet \\
        In Satan's subtle snare; \\
        Unfold Thy way of unity, \\
        Show them the one Community.

    \item All heaven shall soon rejoice \\
        (Oh, haste the blissful day), \\
        When Thou shalt call Thine own \\
        From every land away, \\
        And those now sundered then shall be \\
        United for eternity.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 410}}
    \\
    \textit{7.6.7.6.D ELLACOMBE}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item How perfect our salvation \\
        Through Him who bore the shame. \\
        We're free from condemnation; \\
        Sin offering He became. \\
        We are a holy nation, \\
        And come in His blest name \\
        To offer our oblation, \\
        To celebrate His fame.

    \item Each Lordly morn confessing \\
        The One who bore our blame, \\
        Such wealth in Him possessing, \\
        His matchless worth acclaim. \\
        Yet week-day griefs oppressing, \\
        With many a weary care, \\
        Oft trials so hard, distressing, \\
        Near drive us to despair.

    \item But aye let us endeavour \\
        To go to God in prayer, \\
        For sure no trial can sever \\
        Our spirits from His care. \\
        Our hardest path is never \\
        So hard as that He trod, \\
        Who day by day is ever \\
        Just leading us to God.

    \item Then sing we our salvation \\
        As patiently we plod, \\
        Resisting all temptation, \\
        Nor fainting 'neath the rod. \\
        With peaceful preparation \\
        For gospel message shod, \\
        We'll seek Christ's approbation, \\
        And tread the path He trod.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 411}}
    \\
    \textit{8.8.6.8.8.6. HULL}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FROM various cares my heart retires; \\
        Though deep and boundless its desires, \\
        I've now to please but One; \\
        Him to whom every knee shall bow, \\
        With Him is all my business now, \\
        And those that are His own.

    \item With these my happy lot is cast, \\
        Through the world's deserts rude and waste, \\
        Or through its gardens fair; \\
        Whether the storms of trouble sweep, \\
        Or all in dead supineness sleep, \\
        T' advance be all my care.

    \item O Lord, my way, my truth, my life, \\
        Henceforth let sorrow, doubt and strife \\
        Drop off like autumn leaves. \\
        Henceforth, as privileged by Thee, \\
        Simple and undistracted be \\
        My soul, which to Thee cleaves.

    \item Let me my feebleness recline \\
        On that eternal love of Thine, \\
        And human thoughts forget; \\
        Childlike attend what Thou wilt say, \\
        Go forth and serve Thee while 'tis day, \\
        Nor leave my sweet retreat.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 412}}
    \\
    \textit{C.M. ROSSINI}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item As pants the hart for cooling streams \\
        When heated in the chase, \\
        So longs my soul, O God, for Thee \\
        And Thy refreshing grace.

    \item For Thee, my God, the living God, \\
        My thirsty soul doth pine; \\
        Oh, when shall I behold Thy face, \\
        Thou Majesty divine?

    \item Why restless, why cast down, my soul? \\
        Hope still, and thou shalt sing \\
        The praise of Him who is thy God, \\
        Thy health's eternal spring.

    \item Oh, send Thy light and send Thy truth, \\
        And let them guide me still \\
        To Zion's mount where Thou dost dwell, \\
        Yea, to Thy holy hill.

    \item Unto the altar of my God, \\
        To God my joy supreme, \\
        I would draw nigh through precious blood, \\
        Where I may worship Him.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 413}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. MONTGOMERY}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item How pleasant, how divinely fair, \\
        O Lord of hosts, Thy dwellings are! \\
        With long desire my spirit faints \\
        To join th' assemblies of Thy saints.

    \item My flesh would rest in Thine abode, \\
        My panting heart cries out for God; \\
        My God, my King, why should I be \\
        So far from all my joys and Thee?

    \item The sparrow chooses where to rest, \\
        And for her young provides her nest; \\
        But will my God to sparrows grant \\
        That pleasure, and let His saints want?

    \item Blest are the saints that find a place \\
        Within the temple of Thy grace; \\
        There they behold Thy gracious rays, \\
        And seek Thy face and learn Thy praise.

    \item Yea, blest are they whose hearts are set \\
        To find the way to Zion's gate; \\
        God is their strength, and on the road \\
        They lean upon their Helper, God.

    \item Cheerful they walk with growing strength, \\
        Till all shall meet in heaven at length; \\
        There, never more to weep or fear, \\
        Before Thy face shall each appear.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 414}}
    \\
    \textit{L.M. RIVAULX}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item THIS is the day the Lord has made, \\
        In it He has His grace displayed. \\
        'Tis ours to sing in holy lays, \\
        For all His love, our grateful praise.

    \item O Lord, for all the days gone by, \\
        Thy name we gladly magnify; \\
        For Thou hast brought us to Thine hill, \\
        Thy purpose, Lord, here to fulfil.

    \item Within Thy house, in number few, \\
        We seek Thy grace Thy will to do. \\
        Lord, of ourselves we're very weak; \\
        Thy help and strength we humbly seek.

    \item The future, Lord, we do not know, \\
        But seek that we in grace may grow, \\
        To meet what comes in simple faith, \\
        Believing what the Scripture saith.

    \item For those who in Jehovah trust, \\
        Triumph they will and triumph must. \\
        Lord God Almighty, Thou our rock, \\
        Wilt surely save Thy little flock.

    \item Oh, quickly send the coming day \\
        When we shall leave these clods of clay; \\
        With those in Christ raised from the dead, \\
        We'll join with joy our glorious Head.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\thispagestyle{empty}
\vspace*{3.5cm}
\begin{center}
    {\LARGE \textbf{SECTION 17}}\\[0.8em]
    {\Large \textsc{The Lord's Future Purposes}}
\end{center}
\vspace{3.5cm}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 415}}
    \\
    \textit{9.8.6.8.8.6. PEMBROKE}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item He sits exalted on the throne, \\
        To us as mighty Saviour known, \\
        Our one and only Lord; \\
        He waits with keen, expectant gaze, \\
        The coming of that day of days, \\
        The day of His reward!

    \item Without delay He then shall rise, \\
        And loud, triumphant through the skies \\
        His gladsome shout will sound;; \\
        Archangel voice and trump of God \\
        Shall waken saints beneath the sod, \\
        The spacious world around, \\
        Dosis G \\
        A bad 2 meso,

    \item In bodies incorrupt shall stand, \\
        In this and many another land, \\
        Those who in Christ have died; \\
        We too, who then alive remain, \\
        Shall see an end of all our pain, \\
        Immortal, glorified.

    \item The Master's mighty, quickening voice \\
        Will make our every heart rejoice, \\
        And then His face we'll see; \\
        *Twas once for us all bruised and marred, \\
        His brow with cruel thorn was scarred; \\
        They nailed Him to the tree!

    \item Before the holy judgment throne \\
        We'll see, as we in awe bow down, \\
        Our works in fire be tried, \\
        In view of that vedi flame, \\
        Be this our prayer, and this our aim \\
        ``In Him may we abide.'' Amen.

    \item The Lord's Future Purposes

    \item But then His face in glory bright \\
        Shall fill our wondering, ravished sight, \\
        And we like Him shall be. \\
        Like Him in all those lovely traits, \\
        Which in His lowly, earthly days \\
        So beautiful we see.

\end{enumerate}

\newpage
\begin{center}
    {\Large \textbf{HYMN 416}}
    \\
    \textit{S.M. DENNIS}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item Tre Lord Himself shall come \\
        And shout the quickening word; \\

    \\
    \textit{L.M. WAVERTREE}
\end{center}
\vspace{1em}

\begin{enumerate}
    \item FOR MORNING USE \\
        New every morning is Thy love, \\
        Our wakening and uprising prove: \\
        Through sleep and darkness safely brought, \\
        Restored to life and power and thought. \\
        New mercies each returning day \\
        Hover around us while we pray; \\
        New perils past, new sins forgiven, \\
        New thoughts of God, new hopes of heaven.

    \item If on our daily course our mind \\
        Be set to hallow all we find, \\
        New treasures still of countless price, \\
        God will provide for sacrifice. \\
        The trivial round, the common task, \\
        Will furnish all we need to ask; \\
        Room to deny ourselves, a road \\
        To bring us daily nearer God.

    \item Only, O Lord, in Thy deep love, \\
        Fit us for perfect rest above; \\
        And help us this and every day \\
        To live more nearly as we pray. \\
        FOR EVENING USE

\end{enumerate}
